─── src/models/qwen3_5/execution/qwen4_expert_pager.h:26-26 ───
[documentation · medium] This class comment contradicts the constructor's own invariant and the
`acquire_staged` doc below it. The constructor rejects any `bank_slot` where `bank_slot_ +
kBankSlots != cache_.slots` with the message "staged banks must be the top of the expert cache
slots", i.e. the banks *overlay* the top cache slots (and the pool's divisor tail) rather than
sitting past the pool. Consequently a decode-time cache-route `resolve` can evict into slot range
`[bank_slot_, cache_.slots)`, and the staged route *does* have to reclaim residency first — as
stated a few lines below in the `acquire_staged` doc: "`forward_start` marks the first staged call
of a forward: it reclaims the bank slots." Please reword this paragraph so it matches the
implementation (banks overlay the top cache/lend slots and are reclaimed at `forward_start`),
otherwise the two statements in this header give contradictory guidance to callers.



─── src/models/qwen3_5/execution/qwen4_expert_pager.h:39-39 ───
[test · low] No test file in the repository references `Qwen4ExpertPager` (only production call
sites in `qwen4_text.cpp`, `program_impl.*`, and the model docs mention it). The class carries
several boundary contracts that are easy to break silently — `columns == max_resolve_columns()` (the
`>` check in `resolve`), the `forward_start` reclaim path, the `lend`/`return_lend` slot-range
transitions, and the capture-mode `resident_valid_ == false` path. Please add unit coverage that
exercises at least the exact-boundary `resolve` call and the lend/return_lend round trip.

─── src/models/qwen3_5/execution/qwen4_workspace.h:72-72 ───
[bug · low] Derived row widths are computed in `std::int32_t` arithmetic before being handed to
`alloc`, so the overflow guard that `dimension()` applies to the individual config values (`qw`,
`kw`) does not cover the composite expressions. Here `2 * qw + 2 * kw` is evaluated as `int` (signed
overflow is UB) and the same pattern appears in the other roots: `2 * heads` and `conv + vw`
(`qwen4_gdn`), `2 * width` (`qwen4_moe`), `wide + h` and `rank + inject_rows`
(`qwen4_ple`/`qwen4_hc`), and `2 * ops::kOffloadMoeTopK * group + 1` (`qwen4_moe`). Since
`dimension()` saturates its argument at `INT32_MAX`, a large config here silently wraps into a
negative/small alloc size instead of raising `std::overflow_error`. Prefer widening the arithmetic
(e.g. `std::int64_t`) or using the project's `artifact::checked_mul` (used for derived widths in
`config.cpp`) for these composite row counts.

─── src/models/qwen3_5/execution/qwen4_workspace.h:124-126 ───
[maintainability · low] This mirrors the runtime bound `std::min(T, experts.max_resolve_columns())`
(`qwen4_text.cpp`, the MoE cache-route loop) but drops the second clamp,
`ops::moe_experts_max_columns(slots)`, which the pager applies for pools larger than 4096 slots. The
result is only over-allocation (the `misses` extent is `2*K*group + 1`, always >= the runtime
requirement), so it is not a correctness hazard, but the duplicated formula can silently drift from
the pager. Consider reusing/asserting against `max_resolve_columns()` and documenting that the
returned group is an upper bound. Note also that `std::max(1, ...)` hides a `slots <
kOffloadMoeTopK` mis-sizing; the comment above states the invariant but nothing enforces it.

─── src/models/qwen3_5/frontend/tokenizer.cpp:1112-1117 ───
[bug · medium] The first matching candidate is accepted and the loop breaks. Candidates are bucketed
per first byte in `added_tokens_` order, which is ascending token id (the vector is sorted by id in
`merge_added_tokens_decoder`). When one added token's content is a prefix of another, the
lower-id/shorter token therefore shadows the longer one, whereas the reference tokenizer resolves
overlapping added tokens by taking the longest match. Consider tracking the longest matching
candidate here instead of stopping at the first match.

─── src/models/qwen3_5/frontend/processor.cpp:114-114 ───
[bug · medium] The clamp is computed from the *unpadded* frame count while the bound it enforces was
checked against `padded_frames`. For odd frame counts (e.g. `frames == 1`, `padded_frames == 2`) the
rescale factor is too small, so the rescaled volume can still exceed `max_pixels` by up to
`padded_frames / frames` (nearly 2x for a single frame): with h = w = 1000 and max_pixels = 1.5e6,
`padded_frames * h * w == 2e6 > 1.5e6`, yet the resulting h' = w' = 1216 gives a volume of ~2.96e6,
well over the budget. Consequence: a video that should have been downscaled to fit is instead
rejected later by `enforce_media_resource_limits` (BudgetExceeded) or consumes an oversized decode
budget. Use the same frame count as the volume check in `beta` (i.e. `padded_frames`) so the
invariant `padded_frames * h * w <= max_pixels` is actually restored, or document why the unpadded
count is intentional.



─── src/models/qwen3_5/frontend/processor.cpp:840-844 ───
[bug · medium] Two problems with this control passed into `media_cache_->begin_prepare`:
1) The caller's `control.cancellation` is dropped, so external cancellation (e.g. client disconnect)
is only observed at the main-thread loop boundaries, never inside in-flight decode/resize work.
2) A request-local flag is injected as the builder's cancellation source. Media builds are keyed by
content digest and shared across concurrent requests (singleflight), so the builder may be executed
on behalf of *another* request. If this request aborts (its own budget/limit failure sets
`stop_preparation`), the shared flight records a cancellation error that is then re-thrown to the
other waiters, failing requests whose media was perfectly valid.
Prefer propagating `control.cancellation` for non-shared work, and keep request-local aborts out of
the shared flight (e.g. publish per-request abort state outside the builder, or let the cache
distinguish producer-failure from waiter-cancellation).

`ProcessorError(ProcessorErrorKind::InvalidMedia, ...)` (and the decode layer's `InvalidInput` is
mapped to `InvalidMedia` by `throw_decode_error`). Callers can therefore not classify an
unprocessable image/video consistently. Consider using
`ProcessorError(ProcessorErrorKind::InvalidMedia, ...)` here as well (same for the video
aspect-ratio and minimum-size checks).

─── src/models/qwen3_5/execution/qwen4_text.cpp:295-296 ───
[bug · low] This linear copy ignores the record view's geometry: `records.conv` is a slice along the

─── src/models/qwen3_5/frontend/tool_call_parser.cpp:669-670 ───
[performance · low] Every failed candidate restarts a full `QwenToolRegionParser` over the remaining
text, and each attempt scans from its own marker to the end of the response. Output containing many
`<tool_call>` occurrences (quoted examples, echoed tool results, code blocks) therefore costs
O(occurrences x response length) on the generation hot path. Consider resuming the scan from the
position where the previous attempt failed, or aborting the retry loop after the first
`TrailingContent` failure (a later marker can only be a quoted example once a full region has
already been left behind).



─── src/models/qwen3_5/frontend/tool_call_parser.cpp:418-420 ───
[bug · low] `find_parameter_close` is purely lexical: a `</parameter>` occurring inside a quoted
JSON string is taken as the parameter terminator. A legal value such as `{"s": "</parameter>"}`
therefore ends the parameter early, the region fails to parse, and the tool call silently degrades
into plain content. The constrained parser is quote-aware for exactly this case (`json_value()`
tracks `quoted`/`escaped`), so consider skipping quoted/escaped spans here too (at least for
parameters whose declared encoding is JSON) to keep the two parsers consistent.

─── src/models/qwen3_5/program/speculative/mtp_alignment.h:27-29 ───

─── src/models/qwen3_5/program/speculative/mtp_alignment.h:53-57 ───

semantics are easy to get wrong, and the defaulted `operator==` below compares `std::nullopt !=
false`, so two projections that are semantically equivalent can compare unequal. Consumers currently
avoid this only by always using `x && *x` / `x.value_or(...)` (see pressure.cpp:2160-2167,
2533-2540). An explicit tri-state enum (e.g. `enum class ForkRequirement { Unspecified, NotRequired,
Required }`) would make the intent self-documenting and keep equality meaningful.

─── src/models/qwen3_5/program/planning/pressure_planner.h:66-66 ───
[bug · medium] `append` is declared `noexcept` but calls `planning_direction_index()`, which is not
`noexcept` and throws `std::logic_error` for an unrecognized direction. Since every helper in this
header (`materialization_machine_work`, `recovery_alternative_work`), and this method itself, are
`noexcept`, that exception cannot propagate — it invokes `std::terminate`. This silently converts an
intended diagnostic into an unrecoverable crash, even though the callers in pressure_planner.cpp are
not `noexcept` and could otherwise translate the error. Either drop `noexcept` from `append` and the
helpers that call it, or make `planning_direction_index` `noexcept` and document/handle invalid
directions without throwing.

─── src/models/qwen3_5/program/speculative/suffix_drafter.h:68-68 ───
[bug · high] The empty-slot sentinel is placed in bit 0 of `key` ("0 marks an empty slot"). Because
the open-addressing probe derives the bucket index from the low bits of this same key, reserving bit
0 forces **every** probe onto an odd bucket: only half of `table_` is ever used, so the intended
load factor of 0.5 becomes ~1.0 and `full_` trips at roughly half the history the capacity was sized
for (the index silently stops accepting keys much earlier than documented). The comment is also
inconsistent with the implementation ("hash + 1" vs. an actual `| 1`). Derive the index from the
shifted key (e.g. mask `key >> 1`) or separate occupancy from the hash (a distinct flag/`count == 0`
check) so the sentinel does not consume an index bit.

─── src/models/qwen3_5/program/speculative/mtp_draft_policy.h:65-65 ───
[documentation · low] The doc comment for `expected_tokens` does not match the estimator that is
actually implemented. It claims `Positions never tested inherit the deepest measured conditional
acceptance`, but the implementation builds a pooled acceptance over all positions and shrinks each
position toward that pool (`(accepted_[j] + kShrinkWeight * pooled) / (tested_[j] +
kShrinkWeight)`). For example a lane observed as `observe(7, 3)` leaves position 3's conditional
acceptance at 0, yet untested positions 4-6 come out near the pooled ≈0.75, not 0. Please reword the
comment to describe the pooled/shrinkage behaviour (or change the estimator) so the header contract
matches the code.

─── src/models/qwen3_5/program/prefix_identity.h:28-28 ───
[maintainability · medium] `matches()` only compares `token_types`, `positions`, vision items and
rewrite frontiers — it deliberately does NOT compare token ids (those live in the separate `ledger`
span). It is therefore only safe when combined with the token comparison performed by

isolation and incorrectly accept a prompt whose token ids differ but whose metadata coincides. Its
only current caller is `prefix_matches()` in the .cpp, so consider narrowing visibility (private +
friend) or documenting the precondition that callers must also compare token ids.

─── src/models/qwen3_5/program/context_work.cpp:306-308 ───

[maintainability · medium] `kv_stream_pages` is byte-for-byte identical to `kv_pages_for_tokens` in
`program_impl.h` (both live in namespace `ninfer::models::qwen3_5::detail`) and both compute

rounding rule ever changes). Since `program_impl.h` already includes this header, consider having
`kv_pages_for_tokens` delegate to `kv_stream_pages` (or move the shared helper into this header) so
the page-rounding logic lives in exactly one place.



─── src/models/qwen3_5/program/planning/kv_stream.h:7-7 ───
[maintainability · low] This header is pure constexpr geometry and only needs the single constant
`kPagedKVPageSize`, but `core/paged_kv_cache.h` transitively pulls in `<cuda_runtime_api.h>`. That

─── src/models/qwen3_5/program/graph_execution.h:17-17 ───
[bug · medium] Implicit invariant with no enforcement: `capture_graph()` resets the arena
immediately before capturing, so every prepared graph has its scratch baked in at the arena base
(`[work.base(), work.base()+N)`). Nothing on this launch path guards that region, so any allocation
made from `state.execution.work` between the capture-time reset and this launch (or earlier in the
same round) occupies the same addresses and is silently clobbered by the replayed graph — a data
corruption that is very hard to diagnose. Please make the precondition explicit, e.g. assert it here
(`state.execution.work.used() == 0`) or at least document that callers must not touch the workspace
arena before launching a prepared executable.



─── src/models/qwen3_5/program/graph_execution.h:26-26 ───
[maintainability · medium] Undocumented side effect on program-wide state: this helper resets
`state.execution.work` for the caller. That reset is what keeps repeated captures from exhausting
the arena and keeps the captured pointers anchored at the arena base, but the contract is invisible
from the signature — a caller that still holds arena tensors captured before the call will have them
silently overwritten by the capture body's allocations (and by every subsequent graph replay).
Please add a comment stating the reset's purpose and that the caller's own pre-capture arena
contents are discarded.



─── src/models/qwen3_5/program/graph_execution.h:19-19 ───
[maintainability · low] This helper is shared by the ordinary, MTP, EAGLE3 and DFlash batch bodies,
but the eager span is always tagged with `Decode`/`decode.eager`. Elsewhere the model code uses the
`Mtp`, `DFlash` and `Eagle3` categories/names for those rounds, so eager MTP/EAGLE3/DFlash spans
will be mis-coloured and mis-attributed in profiles. Consider letting the caller pass the
name/category (defaulting to the current values) or at least noting the simplification.



─── src/models/qwen3_5/program/speculative/suffix_drafter.cpp:52-52 ───
[performance · medium] `key | 1ULL` forces the stored key's bit 0 to 1. Since the home bucket is
`key & mask_` and `mask_ == entries - 1` is odd, every home index will be odd: only `entries/2`
slots can ever serve as a hash home. With `entries == 2 * indexed` and up to `indexed - 2` trigrams
stored at nominal capacity, the load on the *usable* home buckets is `(indexed-2)/(entries/2) ~=
1.0`, not the `<= 0.5` the constructor comment promises. Linear probing then degenerates into long
probe chains and `full_` gets set (silently dropping recall) far earlier than intended. Guarantee
non-zero without collapsing the low bit, e.g. remap zero to a sentinel.

-     return key | 1ULL;
+     // Remap the (astronomically rare) zero mix result to a nonzero sentinel instead of forcing
+     // bit 0: OR-ing 1 makes every home index odd, halving the number of usable buckets.
+     return key == 0 ? 1ULL : key;


─── src/models/qwen3_5/program/speculative/suffix_drafter.cpp:77-77 ───

diverge: `sync` keeps inserting past the table's designed load factor, so the table can fill and set
`full_` (losing recall) and `history_` reallocates despite the reserve. Store the sizing bound (e.g.
an `index_limit_` member initialised to the same `min(...)`) and use it both for `reserve` and for
the stop condition here.

-         if (indexed_ >= kMaxIndexedTokens) {
+         if (indexed_ >= index_limit_) {

─── src/models/qwen3_5/program/grammar_masks.cpp:25-25 ───

─── src/models/qwen3_5/program/planning/pressure_planner.cpp:405-405 ───
[bug · medium] `populate_options` mutates shared state (`options.victims[i].decisions`,
`eviction_choice`, `preserve_choice`) inside this loop but only sets `options.populated = true`
after the loop completes. If any iteration throws (the `inspect_*` calls and the `"...has no maximal
outcome"`/`"victim owner is invalid"` logic_errors), previously processed victims keep their pushed
decisions while `populated` stays false. A later call for the same candidate (e.g. another target
for it) re-enters the loop and appends duplicate eviction/preserve decisions, silently corrupting
the victim decision indices and therefore every choice ordinal. Make the population atomic (populate
into temporaries and swap in, or clear/roll back victim state in a catch) before the guard can be
re-entered with a partially filled state.

─── src/models/qwen3_5/program/storage/host_kv_store.h:596-598 ───
[bug · high] `partition_extent` mutates the source extent *before* calling operations that can
throw. `original.allocation` is moved out/reset and `original.generation` is incremented, and only
then does the loop call `arena_->split(...)`, which can throw (`std::invalid_argument` for a stale
allocation, `std::out_of_range`, and notably `std::logic_error` "Host KV descriptor capacity
invariant was violated" when the arena's descriptor capacity is exhausted mid-split).
`arena_->page_unit(...)` can also throw `std::out_of_range`. If any of those throw, the local
`remaining` is destroyed (returning the allocation to the arena) while `extents_[index]` is left
with `state == Published` but an empty `allocation`, a bumped generation (so the old capability is
now stale and `release()`/`require()` can no longer fix it), and memberships whose pages still hold
host replicas pointing at released memory. `release_page_replicas()` propagates such exceptions to
the caller with the store corrupted. Perform all splits first (collect the pieces into a local
buffer) and only commit mutations to `extents_`/memberships once no further throwing call remains,
or at minimum catch and restore the invariant.



─── src/models/qwen3_5/program/storage/host_kv_store.h:106-108 ───
[bug · medium] `prepare()` has no cleanup path for partial failure. Once `arena_->allocate` succeeds
and the descriptor is popped from `free_`, every later statement can throw (`take_membership`'s
`memberships_` access, `pages.content_epoch/committed_columns`, and finally `pages.pin_source(page)`
which can throw for an invalid handle). On such a throw the Reserved extent, its allocation, the
consumed membership nodes and `free_count_` are all leaked/left dangling, and pages already pinned
in the earlier iterations of the final loop are never unpinned. Wrap the post-allocation work so
that a failure un-pins the already-pinned pages and calls `release_descriptor` before rethrowing (or
convert it to a `std::nullopt` return).



─── src/models/qwen3_5/program/storage/host_kv_store.h:96-98 ───
[bug · low] `membership` is not checked for duplicate logical pages. A duplicate passes both the
`can_pin_*` loop and the capacity check, and `pin_source` is called twice for the same page, but
`attach_host_replica` in `publish()` will then fail for the second occurrence and hit
`std::terminate()`. Reject duplicates (or reject a page that is already host-resident) here so the
failure surfaces as `std::nullopt` instead of a hard crash.



─── src/models/qwen3_5/program/storage/host_kv_store.h:262-263 ───
[bug · low] The `release()` validation omits the content check that `partition_extent()` performs:

─── src/models/qwen3_5/program/storage/host_kv_store.h:707-708 ───
[bug · medium] Thread-safety/reentrancy hazard: these query-style `const` methods
(`can_release_page_replicas`, `can_allocate_after_page_releases`, `release_page_replicas`) write to
`mutable` scratch state (`release_stamp_`, `release_marks_`, `extent_marks_`, `affected_extents_`,
`suballocation_scratch_`). Two calls on the same store — even two "read-only" `can_*` calls, or a
`can_*` call made while a `release_*` operation is in flight — interleave their stamps/marks and
silently produce wrong results (and a stale `affected_extents_` list). Because the methods are
`const` and named like queries, callers will reasonably assume they are safe to invoke concurrently.
Either document the single-threaded/non-reentrant requirement explicitly or move the scratch buffers
to the stack/local `thread_local` storage.

─── src/models/qwen3_5/program/planning/startup.cpp:1273-1275 ───

─── src/models/qwen3_5/program/round_buffers.cpp:334-336 ───
[bug · low] This guard compares only the total byte count, which is not sufficient to guarantee that
a narrower view aliases the intended data. The regions are declared as `{..., columns, batch}` while
the views use `{..., width, batch}` (`columns >= width`); because the middle extent (not the
trailing `batch`) differs, the first `width * batch` elements of a `{columns, batch}` region are not
the `{width, batch}` sub-block for any row beyond the first. As written, the narrowing path is only
correct when the state's window equals the layout's window (which is what `RoundState` currently
passes). If the documented use case (`several states over one backing serve graphs captured at
different draft lengths`) is intended, please assert/document that the layout and state windows must
match, or make the check verify the inner extents rather than the raw byte count.



─── src/models/qwen3_5/program/storage/kv_store.h:1003-1003 ───
[bug · medium] Asymmetric reservation accounting between `prepare_activation` and
`commit_activation`. Here the reservation is sized from `required_pages` (the activation frontier's
page count), but `commit_activation` recomputes the expected size as
`activation.requested_entitlement_ - (address.page_count - streamed)` and requires exact equality.
`ensure_mapped_to_tokens` explicitly allows a speculative mapping to extend beyond the stage's
needs, so `address.page_count > pages_for_tokens(*activation_frontier)` is a legal state. In that
case the extra mapped pages are Device-resident (commit requires it) and are not streamable, so
`streamed` is identical in both computations while the base differs by `(page_count -
required_pages)`; `commit_activation` then always throws `KV activation capacity reservation
changed` and the activation can never succeed.

The entitlement semantics documented on `prepare_activation` ("the entitlement then covers the
Device-resident members only") match `commit_activation`, so the base used here should be the full
mapped membership. Note that the `streamed`/`missing_replicas` loops above must use the same base to
stay consistent.

-         const std::uint32_t device_pages = required_pages - streamed;
+         const std::uint32_t device_pages = address.page_count - streamed;


─── src/models/qwen3_5/program/storage/kv_store.h:1087-1089 ───
[bug · medium] `commit_activation` transfers ownership (marking the address active and clearing
`activation.owner_`) before the fallible steps that follow (`retain_active_reference` loop and
`tables_->publish` inside `publish_membership`). If either throws, the address is left
occupied+active while holding the moved-in reservation and execution row, and the activation object
no longer owns them (no cleanup in its destructor). `create_active`'s handler then calls
`(void)release(*handle)`, which is a no-op for an active address (`can_release` returns false when
`address.active`), so the address-space slot, its Device capacity reservation, and its execution row
leak silently.

Suggestion: keep the address non-active and keep `owner_` set until all fallible work is done (do
the retain/publish first, transfer ownership and set `active` last), or make `create_active`'s
failure path undo an already-active address (e.g. via `release_after_deactivate`) before rethrowing.



─── src/models/qwen3_5/program/storage/kv_store.h:619-621 ───
[maintainability · low] `can_retain_reference` is stricter than the mutator it guards: with `writer
== true` it additionally demands `page.references == 0`, while `retain_reference(handle, true)` only
rejects `writer_references != 0` and happily increments `references` when it is non-zero. A caller
that pre-validates with the predicate can be told a state is illegal even though the mutation would
succeed, and `can_retain_reference(..., true)` currently has no callers in this file (all uses pass
`false`), so the mismatch is easy to miss. Make the predicate and the mutator agree on the
preconditions for taking the writer role.



─── src/models/qwen3_5/program/storage/kv_store.h:1449-1457 ───
[bug · low] Partial source-pin leak on the exception path. The `catch` correctly aborts the staged
tail destination, but it does not undo the `pin_source` calls already performed by the loop above:
if `pin_source` throws (handle validation or pin-count overflow) after some pages were pinned, those
pins are left behind and the destination address is never activated, so the members can never be
unpinned by a later commit or abort. The same pattern exists in `prepare_prefix_fork`, where the pin
loop runs after the tail destination has been materialized with no rollback. Track how many pages
were pinned and unpin them in the handler (or pin before allocating anything fallible).



─── src/models/qwen3_5/program/storage/kv_store.h:1925-1927 ───
[performance · low] `release()` calls `rebuild_checkpoint_protection()`, which rescans every
occupied address space and every one of its member pages (twice, with a validating
`set_protected_coverage` per page). `release()`/`release_after_deactivate` run per request
completion, so this turns an O(1) slot free into an O(addresses x page_capacity) sweep on a hot
path, even when no address currently has a checkpoint frontier. Consider short-circuiting when no
occupied address has a non-zero `checkpoint_frontier` (e.g. keep a counter/flag), or rebuilding only
the pages belonging to the address being released.



─── src/models/qwen3_5/program/speculative/mtp_draft_policy.cpp:37-39 ───
[bug · medium] Bound inconsistency: this validation (and the matching `ladder_.back() >

`kLookupDecodeMaximumDrafts` (15), but `MtpAcceptanceEstimate::observe`/`expected_tokens` clamp
every `k` to `kMtpDecodeMaximumDrafts` (7) and the `tested_`/`accepted_` arrays are only 7 wide. A
policy built with any rung in [8, 15] would have its token estimate silently saturate at the rung-7

the ladder here by `kMtpDecodeMaximumDrafts`, or size the estimate storage to

─── src/models/qwen3_5/measurement.cpp:63-66 ───
[bug · high] The inventory only serializes `view.parts`, but weights with `Residency::HostFile` are
materialized with an **empty** `view.parts` (see `loading::resolve_weights`:
`WeightView{item.reference.shape, {}}`, with the geometry moved into `BoundWeight::mapped_geometry`

─── src/models/qwen3_5/program/planning/rebuild_work.h:30-34 ───
[bug · medium] The single-split `chunks_for` arithmetic silently assumes two invariants that are
never checked:
1) `tail_begin` is exactly one of the split points used to produce `work.chunks` (which comes from
`segmented_prefill_chunks` in `request_plan.cpp`, built with the admission-time capture/rewrite
boundary list and the admission-time `prefill_chunk`), and
2) no new segment boundary is introduced inside `(previous_frontier, frontier]`.

If either is violated (a stale `tail_begin`, a boundary minted while the frontier advances — e.g. a
new rewrite/execution split added by `commit_generated_prefix_identity` just before
`advance_rebuild_work` — or a different `prefill_chunk` than the one the work was built with), the
counters drift away from `rebuild_work_at_frontier(prompt, frontier, ...)` for the same frontier.
The guard below only detects underflow (`work.chunks < old_tail_chunks`), so an over-count or an
under-count is silent and corrupts the prefill cost model. Consider taking the boundary list and
recomputing via `segmented_prefill_chunks`, or at minimum documenting/asserting the preconditions
and returning the updated `tail_begin` so callers can keep the split in sync. The existing test only
exercises the `tail_begin == prompt_tokens` single-boundary case; a case crossing a newly introduced
boundary would be worth adding.



─── src/models/qwen3_5/program/planning/rebuild_work.h:36-37 ───
[bug · low] `work.vision_items`/`work.vision_patches` are carried over unchanged while
`tokens`/`attention_pairs` are recomputed for the wider frontier, so they are only echoed through
`make_prefill_work`. A fresh `rebuild_work_at_frontier` at the same frontier counts every vision
item whose spans are fully consumed by that frontier, so when the work originates from a smaller
frontier (identity/capture frontier, adopted checkpoint) the advanced work under-reports vision
work. Either recompute the vision counters (which requires the prompt/vision spans) or document
explicitly that callers must only pass a work whose vision counters are already final for all
subsequent frontiers.



─── src/models/qwen3_5/program/storage/kv_store.h:1076-1079 ───
[bug · high] Activation reservation accounting is computed over two different page bases.
`prepare_activation` sizes the reservation over `required_pages` (frontier-limited:
`activation_frontier ? pages_for_tokens(*frontier) : address.page_count`), while `commit_activation`
counts `streamed` / derives `device_pages` from the full `address.page_count`. When the address has
mapped pages beyond the activation frontier (permitted by `ensure_mapped_to_tokens`, which only
guarantees committed coverage is a lower bound), the two disagree: prepare under-counts missing
replicas and sizes `growth` on the shorter prefix, so commit rejects the reservation with "KV
activation capacity reservation changed" (and also rejects the extra pages as "unavailable Device
page"). Use the same page base in both paths (either validate only `required_pages`, or size prepare
over `address.page_count`).



─── src/models/qwen3_5/program/storage/kv_store.h:1172-1178 ───
[bug · medium] `prepare_prefix_fork` has no exception rollback, unlike `prepare_active_snapshot`.
After `materialize_transfer_destination` creates a descriptor pinned with `references == 0` /
`destination_pinned == true`, the following `pin_source` loop and the reservation/row hand-off can
still throw (stale membership handle, pin-count overflow). If that happens the reservation object is
never constructed, so `abort_prefix_fork` never runs: the staged tail descriptor stays occupied with
a leaked Device lease and its reservation page is never returned, and any pages pinned earlier in
the loop keep their `source_pins`. Wrap the materialize/pin sequence in a try/catch that aborts the
tail destination and unpins already-pinned sources (mirror `prepare_active_snapshot`).



─── src/models/qwen3_5/program/storage/kv_store.h:1084-1087 ───
[bug · medium] `commit_activation` performs irreversible state changes before the last fallible
step. It transfers ownership of the reservation/row, sets `address.active = true`, and clears
`activation.owner_` before the subsequent `clear_protection`/`set_writer`/`retain_active_reference`
loop and `publish_membership` (which calls `tables_->publish`). If any of those throw, the address
is already active, so `create_active`'s catch-path `release(*handle)` becomes a no-op (`can_release`
returns false for an active address) and the address slot, its Device capacity reservation and its
execution row are leaked, leaving a half-activated address. Publish the membership (or at least keep
the state rollback-able) before committing the active state, or teach the error path to unwind this
case.



─── src/models/qwen3_5/program/storage/kv_store.h:1449-1457 ───
[bug · low] `prepare_active_snapshot`'s catch block aborts the staged tail destination but does not
unpin the source pages already pinned by the loop above it. If `pin_source` throws part-way through,
the reservation object is never constructed, so `abort_active_snapshot` never runs and the earlier
pins leak (subsequent activations/fork checks will see non-zero `source_pins`). Track how many pages
were pinned and unpin them in the catch block.



─── src/models/qwen3_5/program/storage/kv_store.h:1923-1926 ───
[performance · low] `KVAddressSpaceStore::release()` unconditionally calls
`rebuild_checkpoint_protection()`, which first zeroes the protected coverage of every page of every
occupied address and then re-applies it by scanning `pages_for_tokens(checkpoint_frontier)` per
address — an O(addresses × page_count) pass on a per-request release path. The same full rebuild is
also triggered by `destructive_truncate_inactive`, `truncate_inactive_prefix` and
`set_checkpoint_requirement`. Consider updating protection incrementally for the touched address (or
only rebuilding when a checkpoint actually shrank) to avoid the quadratic cost.



─── src/models/qwen3_5/program/storage/kv_store.h:307-308 ───
[maintainability · low] The file uses `std::span` (batch `materialize`, `ensure_mapped_to_tokens`)

pulling it in. (`<array>` appears unused and can be dropped.)



─── src/models/qwen3_5/program/storage/kv_store.h:1076-1076 ───
[bug · high] Reservation-accounting mismatch between `prepare_activation` and `commit_activation`.
`prepare_activation` sizes the Device capacity reservation from the activation-frontier page count
(`required_pages = pages_for_tokens(*activation_frontier)` when a frontier is given, else
`address.page_count`), and computes `device_pages = required_pages - streamed`. Here, commit
validates against the full mapped page count (`device_pages = address.page_count - streamed`), so
`expected` is computed over a larger set of pages. When the address has speculative pages mapped
beyond the committed/activation frontier (a state the code explicitly allows), the prepared
reservation and `expected` disagree and a valid activation fails the `page_reservation_.pages() !=
expected` check (or state drifts). Use the same page base in both paths — e.g. derive the
commit-side page count from `activation.activation_frontier_` exactly as prepare does.



─── src/models/qwen3_5/program/storage/kv_store.h:1087-1089 ───
[bug · medium] The address is switched to `active`, the reservation and execution row are
transferred out of the activation reservation, and `activation.owner_` is cleared *before* the
fallible work that follows (`clear_protection`/`set_writer`/`retain_active_reference` and
`publish_membership`). If any of those throw, the reservation no longer aborts (owner_ is null) and
the cleanup in `create_active`/`activate` — `release(*handle)` — is a no-op for an active address,
leaking the address slot, its Device capacity reservation and the execution row. Perform the state
transition after the fallible publication, or add explicit rollback on this path.



─── src/models/qwen3_5/program/storage/kv_store.h:1338-1340 ───
[bug · medium] `abort_prefix_fork` unpins any source page whose `source_pins != 0`, including pages
that `prepare_prefix_fork` deliberately skipped (streamed / Host-only pages) and therefore never
pinned. If another fork reservation over the same source pinned such a page (a different stream
range makes it non-streamed there), this decrements someone else's pin and corrupts its accounting,
so that reservation's later commit/publish check can spuriously fail. Mirror the prepare-time
streamed predicate (as `commit_prefix_fork` does), or record the exact pages this reservation pinned
and unpin only those.



─── src/models/qwen3_5/program/storage/kv_store.h:1176-1178 ───
[bug · medium] `prepare_prefix_fork` has no exception rollback, unlike `prepare_active_snapshot`
which guards its staging and pin loop with `try/catch`. If `pin_source` throws after
`materialize_transfer_destination` succeeded, the transfer-destination descriptor is left occupied
with `destination_pinned` set and zero references, and any pins already taken on earlier pages are
not released; the local `tail_destination` handle does not abort itself. Wrap the tail
materialization and the pin loop in `try/catch` and call `abort_transfer_destination` / unpin the
already-pinned pages before rethrowing.



─── src/models/qwen3_5/program/storage/kv_store.h:619-621 ───
[bug · low] `can_retain_reference` and `retain_reference` disagree for the writer case (and on
`destination_pinned`): the predicate requires `references == 0` when `writer` is true, but the
mutator only rejects `writer_references != 0` (it also does not check `destination_pinned`). A
caller that pre-validates with the predicate will reject states the mutator would accept, and the
extra `destination_pinned` condition makes the two contracts diverge further. Reconcile the
conditions so the predicate exactly matches what the mutator enforces.



─── src/models/qwen3_5/program/storage/kv_store.h:12-12 ───

─── src/models/qwen3_5/program/storage/kv_store.h:1923-1926 ───
[performance · low] Each `release()` triggers `rebuild_checkpoint_protection()`, which clears
protection for every page of every occupied address and then re-applies it, i.e.
O(occupied_addresses x page_capacity) on every address release (and this also happens on truncate
and requirement changes). For a store holding many addresses this is a per-request hot-path cost;
consider updating only the affected address/checkpoint's pages incrementally instead of a full
rebuild.



─── src/models/qwen3_5/program/storage/kv_store.h:1076-1077 ───
[bug · high] Reservation accounting is inconsistent between `prepare_activation` and
`commit_activation`. `prepare_activation` sizes the reservation over `required_pages` (the
activation-frontier limited count), while `commit_activation` validates `expected` against the full
`address.page_count`. When an activation frontier is supplied and the address holds device-resident
members beyond that frontier (a speculative mapping, which `ensure_mapped_to_tokens` explicitly
allows), the two bases disagree and a legitimate activation is rejected with "KV activation capacity
reservation changed". Use the same page range for counting `streamed`/`device_pages` in both paths
(e.g. iterate `required_pages` here as well, or derive both from the same frontier-limited count).



─── src/models/qwen3_5/program/storage/kv_store.h:1084-1087 ───
[bug · medium] Irreversible state hand-over before a fallible step. The address takes ownership of
the reservation/row, is marked `active`, and `activation.owner_` is cleared before the subsequent
per-page writer/protection updates and `publish_membership` (which can throw, e.g. on table
publication). If any of those fail, the reservation can no longer abort via the reservation object
and `create_active`'s cleanup `release(*handle)` is a no-op for an already-active address, leaking
the address slot, its Device capacity reservation and the execution row. Perform the hand-over after
the fallible publication/updates succeed, or add an explicit rollback path.



─── src/models/qwen3_5/program/storage/kv_store.h:1172-1178 ───
[bug · medium] `prepare_prefix_fork` has no exception rollback, unlike `prepare_active_snapshot`. If

─── src/models/qwen3_5/program/storage/kv_store.h:1452-1457 ───

─── src/models/qwen3_5/program/storage/kv_store.h:1923-1926 ───

─── src/models/registry.cpp:36-36 ───
[maintainability · low] Unreachable/defensive fallback: the switch already covers every enumerator

`-Wswitch` guarantee already proves exhaustiveness) or add a `default:` branch that fails loudly (an

─── src/ops/attn_input_proj/q8/q8_attn_input_plan.h:32-39 ───

─── src/ops/attn_input_proj/q8/q8_attn_input_plan.h:47-47 ───

─── src/ops/dynamic_grouped_conv/bf16/bf16_dynamic_grouped_conv_prepare_kernels.h:13-15 ───
[maintainability · medium] The `partial` scratch buffer has an implicit contract that is not

[bug · medium] `div_up` only implements ceiling division for non-negative operands. Because `T` is
allowed to be signed (only integrality is asserted), negative `x` silently yields wrong results:
`div_up(-4, 3)` returns `0` while ceil(-4/3) == -1, and `div_up(-3, 3)` returns `1` instead of `-1`.
In addition `d == 0` is UB (division/modulo by zero), unlike `align_up`, which validates its
argument at compile time. Please document/assert the `x >= 0 && d > 0` precondition (or restrict `T`
to unsigned) so signed callers are not silently misled.



─── src/ops/common/math.h:24-24 ───
[bug · medium] `round_up` inherits `div_up`'s negative-operand problem (e.g. `round_up(-1, 8)`
returns `8` instead of `0` for signed `T`) and additionally has no protection against `multiple ==
0`, which is a fatal division by zero. Document/assert non-negative `x` and non-zero `multiple`, or
add an explicit guard.



─── src/ops/common/math.h:32-33 ───
[bug · medium] `x + a - 1` is evaluated in type `T`, and the static_assert allows `T` to be signed.
For `T = int` with `x` near `std::numeric_limits<T>::max()` this is signed overflow (UB); for
unsigned `T` it wraps and produces a bogus 0. The host-side counterpart in `core/checked_math.h`
performs a checked add for exactly this reason. Consider computing in an unsigned/wider type, or
explicitly document that `x + Alignment - 1` must not overflow.

-     constexpr T a = static_cast<T>(Alignment);
-     return (x + a - 1) & ~(a - 1);
+     using U = std::make_unsigned_t<T>;
+     constexpr U a = static_cast<U>(Alignment);
+     return static_cast<T>((static_cast<U>(x) + a - 1) & ~(a - 1));


─── src/ops/common/math.h:39-40 ───
[bug · low] `sign_extend` never masks `x` to the low `Bits` bits, yet it replicates bit `Bits - 1`
of the *whole* input. A caller passing a value with higher bits set (e.g. the result of an
arithmetic right shift of a signed value) therefore gets a silently wrong result. Masking first (`x
& ((1 << Bits) - 1)`) or documenting the precondition "all bits above `Bits - 1` must be zero" would
make the contract unambiguous.



─── src/ops/gdn_gating_proj/bf16/bf16_gdn_gating_proj_kernels.h:28-28 ───
[bug · medium] API asymmetry / missing bounds check: this launcher receives both `workspace` and
`workspace_bytes` and validates the buffer (`workspace_bytes < required` -> throw), but every
cooperative MMA launcher below (`..._mma_split8/4/2`, `..._35_mma_split32/16/8/4/2`,
`..._norm_gating_proj_35_mma_split32`) declares only a raw `void* workspace` with no byte count.
Those launchers therefore cannot verify the split-K reduction scratch they write into, so a caller
that under-allocates (or passes the wrong plan's `workspace_bytes`) gets a silent out-of-bounds
write instead of a diagnosable error. Please add a `std::size_t workspace_bytes` parameter to the
cooperative declarations and validate it in the corresponding definitions, matching the small-T
contract.

─── src/models/qwen3_5/program/vision_control.h:20-23 ───

─── src/models/qwen3_5/program/transactions/materialization.cpp:29-31 ───
[bug · medium] `revalidate_materialization` returns `PreflightStatus::StalePolicyState` for
*recoverable* staleness (open/pending transaction, unsettled StateImage fork, blocked Host
allocation, peak no longer fitting, stale slot/generation) and `InvariantFailure` only for genuine
contract violations — `pressure.cpp` treats these two outcomes differently for exactly that reason.
Here both are collapsed into a thrown `std::logic_error`. The analogous reservation path
(`reserve_active_capture_impl` in `capture.cpp`) explicitly returns
`ContextTransactionReserveStatus::Aborted` for the same "another lane holds an unsettled StateImage
fork" condition with the comment "A capture is optional: this lane forgoes it rather than fail the
Engine". Because the throw escapes into the engine loop (which only converts `std::logic_error` into
a request failure plus an OOM-recovery backoff, `engine_core.h` ~L2300), a benign race makes this
request fail instead of being re-planned via the `Aborted` -> `Stale` -> retry path that
`ResourceManager::reserve_materialization` provides. Nothing has been mutated yet at this point, so
returning `Aborted` is safe. Suggest distinguishing the two statuses, e.g.:
```cpp
if (preflight == runtime::PreflightStatus::StalePolicyState) {
    // Benign staleness (e.g. another lane holds an unsettled StateImage fork): let the caller
    // re-plan instead of failing the Engine. Nothing has been staged yet.
    return runtime::ContextTransactionReserveStatus::Aborted;
}
if (preflight != runtime::PreflightStatus::Ready) {
    throw std::logic_error("materialization invariant failed during staging revalidation");
}
```
(adjust the enumerator names if the `PreflightStatus` spelling differs).

─── src/ops/hyper_connection/hyper_connection.cpp:18-18 ───
[performance · medium] These validation helpers build the message string eagerly on every call,
including the success path: each `require(...)` receives a freshly constructed temporary
`std::string(label) + " dtype mismatch"` (16+ chars, so it heap-allocates) that is discarded when
the check passes. Since these wrappers run once per layer per kernel in the forward pass, that is
needless per-call allocation. The house pattern used in `src/ops/wrapper/mtp_round.cpp` /
`src/ops/kv_cache_append.cpp` builds the message only inside the failure branch. Consider making
`require` take the pieces (or a small formatter) and construct the string only when `!condition`.

─── src/ops/hyper_connection/hyper_connection.cpp:88-89 ───

─── src/ops/launcher/gemma_flash_attention.h:37-37 ───
[bug · high] `blocks` can be zero, which makes the later `resident / blocks` an integer division by
zero (SIGFPE/UB). This is reachable: `flash_workspace_bytes` validates
`query_tokens/key_tokens/batch/kv_heads` but never `query_heads`, and `flash_row_tiles` computes
`query_heads / kv_heads`; so for `query_heads == 0` or `query_heads < kv_heads` the group is 0,
`row_tiles` is 0, `blocks` is 0, and with `key_tiles >= 4` the early return is skipped and the
division blows up. Guard the degenerate case here (and/or validate `query_heads >= kv_heads` in
`flash_workspace_bytes`).

      const std::int64_t blocks    = row_tiles * kv_heads * batch;
+     if (blocks <= 0) return 1;


─── src/ops/launcher/gemma_flash_attention.h:88-89 ───
[bug · medium] `params.query_heads / params.kv_heads` is evaluated before any validation of the head
counts. The callers (`sliding_causal_attention_launch`, `causal_compact_attention_launch`) only
validate token/batch counts, and `flash_workspace_bytes` explicitly tolerates `kv_heads <= 0`, so a
`kv_heads <= 0` (or `query_heads < kv_heads`) argument reaches this line and triggers an integer
division by zero instead of a diagnostic. Validate `params.kv_heads > 0`, `params.query_heads >=
params.kv_heads` and `params.query_heads % params.kv_heads == 0` at the top of this function (before
any division) and throw `std::invalid_argument` otherwise.



─── src/ops/launcher/gemma_flash_attention.h:67-71 ───
[bug · medium] `cudaFuncSetAttribute` sets a per-(device, function) property, but the result is
cached in a function-local `static`, so it is only applied for whichever device happens to make the
first call. If the process runs the op on a second device, the attribute is not set there and the
launch with `Config::kBytes` (> 48 KiB default) dynamic shared memory will fail. The same holds for
the `per_sm` occupancy cache below, since blocks-per-SM is architecture dependent. Re-apply the
attribute per device (e.g. key the cache on the current device) instead of caching it once per
process.

─── src/ops/linear/bf16/bf16_config.h:71-72 ───
[maintainability · high] `Bf16SimtSchedule` declares its template parameters in the order
`(PhaseStride, PhaseUnroll, PrefetchDepth, MinBlocksPerSm)`, whereas `Bf16GemvSchedule` above uses
`(PhaseStride, PrefetchDepth, PhaseUnroll, MinBlocksPerSm)`. All of these are plain `int` with
overlapping valid value sets (PrefetchDepth ∈ {1,2}, PhaseUnroll ∈ {1,2,4,8}), so copying/adapting a
schedule specialization from one struct to the other will silently bind the two arguments to the
wrong knobs without any compile error. Please make the two parameter lists consistent (e.g. keep
PhaseStride, PrefetchDepth, PhaseUnroll, MinBlocksPerSm everywhere) so the API cannot be misused by
argument reordering.

-           Bf16WeightCache WeightCache, Bf16PhaseOrder PhaseOrder, int PhaseStride, int PhaseUnroll,
-           int PrefetchDepth, int MinBlocksPerSm>
+           Bf16WeightCache WeightCache, Bf16PhaseOrder PhaseOrder, int PhaseStride,
+           int PrefetchDepth, int PhaseUnroll, int MinBlocksPerSm>

─── src/ops/linear/nvfp4/nvfp4_config.h:16-16 ───
[bug · low] Boundary handling: the rounding is done in signed 32-bit arithmetic and no precondition
is stated or asserted. For `tokens <= 0` the formula silently yields 0 (e.g. tokens = -1 -> 0), and
a caller using the result as a CUDA grid dimension would then launch 0 blocks. Consider documenting
the `tokens > 0` precondition (or asserting on it) so misuse fails loudly rather than producing a
degenerate grid size.



─── src/ops/linear/fp8/fp8_geometry.h:42-42 ───
[maintainability · low] `resolve_fp8_geometry` re-encodes, by hand, the same (output_rows,
input_rows) pairs that are already defined by the type aliases and the enum. Adding/removing a
geometry now requires editing three places (alias, enum, and this if-chain) with no compile-time
guarantee they stay in sync — a missing/typo'd branch silently turns a supported problem into a
runtime `throw`. Consider deriving the lookup from a single source of truth, e.g. a small constexpr
table of {output, input, id} pairs iterated by the resolver.

─── src/ops/launcher/sigmoid_gate_mul.h:17-17 ───
[maintainability · low] The `block` launch-control parameter has no documented contract. The
launcher computes `div_up(packs, block)`, so a value of `0` (or negative) causes a division by

(multiples of 32, <= 256); nothing in this private API states that requirement. Document the valid
range/non-zero precondition (or accept a validated config type) so callers cannot misuse it.

[maintainability · low] Naming is inconsistent with the owning Op: the public contract/op is

─── src/ops/linear/exl3/exl3_dispatch.h:40-41 ───
[bug · high] `exl3_copy_rows` performs no validation of its arguments against the tensor extents.
`source_row`, `rows` and `columns` are signed `int32_t` derived from runtime shapes, and
`cudaMemcpy2DAsync` will not bounds-check them: a negative `source_row`/`rows` underflows when cast
to `std::size_t` (producing a huge width/offset), and `source_row + rows > source.ne[0]` or `rows >
destination.ne[0]` silently reads/writes out of the allocation. Since this is a pitched
device-to-device copy, an out-of-range pitch/width corrupts neighboring memory rather than failing
loudly. Please add assertions (`source_row >= 0`, `rows >= 0`, `source_row + rows <= source.ne[0]`,
`rows <= destination.ne[0]`, `columns >= 0`) before the copy.



─── src/ops/linear/exl3/exl3_dispatch.h:39-39 ───
[bug · medium] The pitch is computed from `ne[0] * sizeof(std::uint16_t)`, which is only correct for
a contiguous tensor. `Tensor` carries real byte strides in `nb[]` (see `set_contiguous_strides`),
and shapes can come from `view`/`slice`/`permute`, so for a non-contiguous view this pitch and the
`source_row` offset (which likewise assumes a 2-byte element stride) are wrong and the copy would
silently read/write the wrong bytes. Use the stride fields instead, e.g. `source.nb[1]` /
`destination.nb[1]` for the pitches and `source.nb[0]` for the row advance.

-         static_cast<std::size_t>(source.ne[0]) * sizeof(std::uint16_t),
+         static_cast<std::size_t>(source.nb[1]),

─── src/ops/linear/fp8/fp8_format.cpp:28-31 ───
[maintainability · medium] These three anonymous-namespace helpers (`checked_mul`, `checked_add`,

weaker than the shared one: it does not validate the alignment, so if it were ever called with

zero). Prefer deleting the local copies and calling the shared

─── src/ops/linear/nvfp4/nvfp4_w4a4_plan.h:24-24 ───
[maintainability · low] The workspace carries only the scale-plane extent, so the launcher can
reject a tiled launch whose plane was sized for fewer tokens than `x.ne[1]` (see the check in
`launch_nvfp4_w4a4_quantize`). The code plane has no such extent: a caller that builds the struct by
hand, or reuses a workspace allocated for a smaller token count, overruns `codes` silently (the
quantizer writes `tokens * K/2` bytes) while the scale overrun is caught. Consider carrying a
`code_bytes` extent here and returning it, and validating it in the same place, so both planes fail
closed.



─── src/ops/linear/nvfp4/nvfp4_dispatch.cpp:28-30 ───
[maintainability · medium] Undocumented, load-bearing contract between the two `uses_a4` call sites.
Here the predicate is queried with the whole interval `(min_tokens, max_tokens)` and the buffer is
sized for `max_tokens`, while `nvfp4_dispatch` queries it with a single token `(x.ne[1], x.ne[1])`
and then allocates for that exact `T`. This is only safe because every registered `uses_a4` is
monotonic in `max_tokens` and ignores `min_tokens` (e.g. `n14336_k5120.cu` returns `max_tokens >=
4`). A future shape whose A4 band is not upward-closed (e.g. "A4 for `T <= 64`, A16 above") would
make the interval query return `true` while `max_tokens` does not itself route to A4 — or, worse,
return `false` for an interval that does contain A4-routed token counts, in which case the capacity
query yields `0` and the subsequent dispatch would run A4 against an unreported/no workspace. Please
document the invariant (`uses_a4(min,max)` must report whether *any* token count in the closed
interval routes to A4, and the reserved bytes must cover the largest such count) directly above the
return, so the predicate's semantics are not left implicit.

+     // uses_a4(min_tokens, max_tokens) must report whether any token count in the closed interval
+     // routes to A4, and the reserved bytes cover the largest such count, so that every dispatch
+     // performed for a T inside the interval finds a workspace large enough for it.
      return allows_a4(policy) && shape.uses_a4(min_tokens, max_tokens)
                 ? nvfp4_w4a4_workspace_capacity_bytes(max_tokens, k)
                 : 0;

─── src/ops/linear/nvfp4/nvfp4_geometry.h:36-36 ───
[bug · medium] The comment above says this alias describes one routed expert's gate/up *pair* ("its
gate rows then its up rows"), but the registered geometry has only 768 output rows, i.e. a single
projection. In the stored bank the pair occupies `2 * 768 = 1536` rows (gate half then up half), and
this matches how the other fused entries in this file are registered (e.g. `N14336K5120` /
`N34816K5120` use the doubled N of a gate/up pair). Because `resolve_nvfp4_geometry` matches on the
exact `(n, k)` of the weight, an alias with `n = 768` can never resolve a per-expert gate/up plane
of 1536 rows, even though `1536 % 128 == 0` so it would satisfy the static asserts. Please confirm
against the artifact: either the template argument should be `Nvfp4Geometry<1536, 2816>` or the
comment must be narrowed to "a single gate (or up) projection".



─── src/ops/linear/nvfp4/nvfp4_geometry.h:53-53 ───

─── src/ops/linear/fp8/fp8_dispatch.cpp:27-28 ───
[performance · low] The pair bump is applied for every `AllowA8Pair` problem regardless of the token
interval. But the fused two-term route is only selected when `x.ne[1] > kFp8A8PairMinTokens` (see
`launch_fp8_a8_tma` in `fp8_launch.cuh`, line 57); below that threshold the dispatch falls back to
the sequential single-term route, which only uses the shape-declared split-K partial plane sized by
`partial_capacity_bytes`. So for any pair-policy interval whose max token count is <=
`kFp8A8PairMinTokens`, `kFp8A8PairPartialBytes` (~21 MiB, i.e. 2 * 170 * 128 * 128 * 4) is reserved
but never touched — both in the workspace-capacity query and in the per-call allocation (which is
called with `x.ne[1]`). Gating the bump on the same threshold the launcher uses keeps the envelope
property while avoiding the waste. (Minor: `std::max` also reads better than the manual larger-of
expression; `#include <algorithm>` would need to be added.)

-     if (!allows_a8_pair(policy)) return declared;
-     return declared > kFp8A8PairPartialBytes ? declared : kFp8A8PairPartialBytes;
+     if (!allows_a8_pair(policy) || max_tokens <= kFp8A8PairMinTokens) return declared;
+     return std::max(declared, kFp8A8PairPartialBytes);

─── src/ops/linear/q8/q8_geometry.h:17-17 ───
[bug · medium] `sizeof(std::uint16_t)` yields `std::size_t` (an unsigned 64-bit type). The usual

obscures intent. Cast the `sizeof` to `std::int32_t` explicitly, or use the literal `2` for the

─── src/ops/linear/q8/q8_operands.h:43-43 ───

twice more across the two vision selectors). The Q5 sibling (`src/ops/linear/q5/shapes/gemma4.cpp`)
factors such a shared ladder into one anonymous-namespace helper, which is what keeps its thresholds
in one place. Consider the same here so a future sweep-driven threshold change cannot be applied to

─── src/ops/linear_pair/q8/q8_pair_kernels.h:10-10 ───
[maintainability · medium] `Q8PairScheduleId` is only forward-declared here, but its definition

─── src/ops/linear_attention/gated_delta_net/launch.h:20-22 ───
[maintainability · medium] The fixed array bound `8` is a magic number that silently duplicates
`kMaximumRecordCapacity` (currently 8) defined in `src/core/gdn_replay_records.cpp`.
`validate_fold_rows()` only rejects `rows.size() > records.spec.record_capacity` and then writes
`packed.row[row]` for every row, so if `kMaximumRecordCapacity` is ever raised above 8 (or the two
values ever drift) this struct would silently overflow. The constraint is only indirectly pinned by
a `static_assert(sizeof(...) == 128)` in `recurrent.cu`, which is fragile. Expose a named
maximum-rows constant (shared with `kMaximumRecordCapacity`) and assert it here so the coupling is
explicit and enforced at this declaration.



─── src/ops/linear_swiglu/nvfp4/nvfp4_linear_swiglu_w4a4_tma_launch.h:20-20 ───

`noexcept`; (2) `output` is expected to be a `[tokens, 17408]` bf16 buffer, and
`weight_codes`/`weight_scales` must be the pre-quantized NVFP4 weights in the layout produced for
the fixed 34816x5120 geometry. Please state these in the header doc so callers do not have to read

─── src/ops/softmax_attention/dense/causal_cache/bf16/instances.h:43-43 ───
[bug · medium] The switch has no `default` case, so any enum value not covered (e.g. an out-of-range
cast, or a newly added instance whose `NINFER_BF16_KV_DESCRIBE` entry was forgotten and only
produced a warning) falls through to this `return {}`. That value-initializes the description to
`query_rows = 0` / `key_rows = 0`. Callers rely on these being positive: `plan.cpp` computes
`ceil_div(rows, description.query_rows)` (division by zero → UB) and builds a partition with
`description.key_rows` as the row granularity. A silent zero result turns a programming error into
undefined behaviour much later. Prefer failing fast (e.g. `assert(false)` / `std::unreachable()` or
throwing) so a missing/unknown instance is reported at the point of misuse.



─── src/ops/softmax_attention/dense/causal_cache/bf16/instances.h:28-28 ───
[maintainability · medium] The `grouped` flag is supplied manually and is not tied to the schedule

cleanly and silently dispatches to the wrong kernel/geometry. Consider deriving the flag from the
schedule (e.g. a schedule-kind trait such as `Schedule::kGrouped`) or adding a `static_assert` that

─── src/ops/linear_topk/linear_topk_workspace.h:39-39 ───
[bug · medium] `rows_per_producer` is used directly as the divisor in `div_up(head_rows,

─── src/ops/linear_topk/linear_topk_workspace.h:42-42 ───
[bug · low] Only a single extra merge level is computed here. The merge kernel's final in-block
reduction can consume at most `kLinearTopKMergeFanIn` (32) input groups (256 threads x 2 items /
kLinearTopK = 32), so the last level must have <= 32 groups. `secondary_groups <= 32` only holds
while `merge_groups <= kLinearTopKMergeFanIn * kLinearTopKMergeFanIn` (i.e. `producer_groups <=
32768`); beyond that the third level is silently omitted and the merge reads only the first 32
groups, producing wrong top-k results with no error. Please enforce this bound explicitly
(throw/assert) so future head-row growth cannot silently corrupt results.

─── src/ops/softmax_attention/common/causal_partition.h:28-28 ───
[bug · medium] `independent_tiles` is used as a divisor without any guard. Integer division by zero
(or a negative value) is undefined behaviour and would crash the host planner. All current call
sites happen to validate `batch >= 1` / `tiles >= 1`, so `independent_tiles` is >= 1 today, but this
is a public `inline constexpr` helper whose precondition is undocumented, and a future/edge caller
passing 0 would be an immediate crash. Please add an explicit guard (the sibling
`causal_partition_target` below has the identical exposure).

+     if (independent_tiles <= 0) return false;
+     const std::int64_t filled =
          (multiprocessor_count / independent_tiles) * static_cast<std::int64_t>(independent_tiles);


─── src/ops/softmax_attention/common/causal_partition.h:34-34 ───
[bug · medium] Same unguarded division as above: `cta_budget / independent_tiles` is UB when
`independent_tiles == 0`. Since this helper centralises the split-target policy for every dtype,
please clamp/validate the denominator here as well (e.g. `if (independent_tiles <= 0) return 1;` or
`std::max(independent_tiles, 1)`) so the contract is enforced in one place.



─── src/ops/softmax_attention/common/causal_partition.h:15-15 ───
[documentation · low] The comment describing `key_shift` is inverted relative to `active()`'s
implementation. Because `active()` uses ceiling division, `count = ceil(visible / 2^key_shift)`, so
each split receives on average *at most* `2^key_shift` keys (the guaranteed lower bound is only
about half of that, e.g. visible=257, key_shift=7 -> 3 splits of ~86 keys). Describing
`1<<key_shift` as the "minimum KV keys per split" can lead readers to assume a work-per-split floor
that the code does not provide; consider wording it as the nominal/maximum keys per split (e.g.
"log2 of the target/max KV keys per split; splits = ceil(visible / 2^key_shift)").

─── src/ops/linear_swiglu/q4/q4_linear_swiglu_plan.cpp:152-154 ───
[maintainability · low] The capacity search relies on the endpoint of each overlapping route being
the widest column in that sub-interval, which is only sound because every workspace size grows
monotonically with `cols`. It additionally relies on `kA8MinCols` coinciding with a *zero-workspace*
route boundary: route `{225,256}` has schedule `MmaSplitHalfPairR32C128` (0 bytes) at `cols == 256`,
so resolving the endpoint `256` to the A8 route cannot hide a non-zero Materialized workspace for
columns `225..255`. If the route table is ever edited so a non-zero-workspace route straddles
`kA8MinCols`, this loop would silently under-size the arena. The nvfp4 sibling guards a comparable
boundary assumption with a `static_assert`; consider adding one here, or documenting the invariant.

─── src/ops/linear_pair/q8/q8_pair_plan.h:74-75 ───
[bug · medium] The plan/problem API only models the pair's *first* operand: `q8_pair_problem` takes

─── src/ops/linear_attention/kimi_delta_attention/kimi_delta_attention.cpp:119-122 ───
[bug · medium] The documented contract (include/ninfer/ops/kimi_delta_attention.h: "Inputs/out are

governed by the state alias rule); the same should be done here.

              "kimi_delta_attention: state input/output may only be disjoint or exactly alias");
+     }

+         for (const Tensor* r : read) {

+         }
+     }

─── src/ops/linear_attention/gated_delta_net/replay.cpp:277-278 ───
[bug · medium] No-op rows are still forced to satisfy the source/destination binding constraints.
`commit_columns == 0` rows are true no-ops in the kernel (`recurrent_fold_kernel` returns
immediately at `if (valid == 0) { return; }` in recurrent.cuh), so such a row never dereferences
`source_state_slot`/`destination_state_slot`. Yet this pairwise loop rejects the whole batch if a
zero-commit row happens to share a slot with another row's binding — and zero-commit rows are
produced in practice (e.g. `prefill.cpp` builds `GdnReplayFoldRow{..., commit_columns = 0}` for
cancelled speculative lanes). Consider skipping rows with `commit_columns == 0` when checking
bindings (they are inert), or explicitly documenting why they must still be disjoint.

─── src/ops/wrapper/cast.cpp:22-24 ───
[bug · low] The alias guard only catches exact pointer equality (`source.data == destination.data`).
Partially overlapping source/destination buffers (e.g. two views into the same allocation with
different offsets) are not detected, yet the write of `destination` would corrupt the still-unread
`source` elements and silently produce wrong results. Consider validating the byte ranges `[data,
data + bytes())` for overlap rather than just pointer equality (consistent with the header contract
"source and destination must not alias").



─── src/ops/wrapper/cast.cpp:18-21 ───
[bug · low] `Tensor::is_contiguous()` returns false whenever any `ne[i] <= 0`, so an empty tensor
(any dimension == 0) is rejected here with the misleading message "tensors must be contiguous and
non-null". Sibling ops (e.g. `gelu`, `rope`) treat empty tensors as a no-op and return early. If
empty shapes can legitimately reach this op (e.g. an empty batch), this throw is a spurious failure;
consider short-circuiting on zero elements before the layout checks.



─── src/ops/wrapper/argmax.cpp:64-66 ───
[bug · high] The public contract documented in include/ninfer/ops/argmax.h requires that `out` must
not overlap `logits`, but this wrapper never validates it. Because the launcher both reads `logits`
and writes `out` in place, aliasing silently corrupts results (the same check is enforced by sibling
wrappers such as target_logprobs.cpp via an `overlaps()` helper). Add the missing non-overlap
validation.

Suggestion: add a helper in the anonymous namespace
```
bool overlaps(const Tensor& lhs, const Tensor& rhs) {
    const auto lhs_begin = reinterpret_cast<std::uintptr_t>(lhs.data);
    const auto rhs_begin = reinterpret_cast<std::uintptr_t>(rhs.data);
    if (lhs_begin <= rhs_begin) { return rhs_begin - lhs_begin < lhs.bytes(); }
    return lhs_begin - rhs_begin < rhs.bytes();
}
```
(requires <cstddef>/<cstdint>) and an explicit check before dispatch.

      if (logits.data == nullptr || out.data == nullptr) {
          throw std::invalid_argument("argmax: logits/out data must be non-null");
+     }
+     if (overlaps(logits, out)) {
+         throw std::invalid_argument("argmax: out must not overlap logits");
      }

─── src/ops/softmax_attention/dense/causal_cache/bf16/split_policy.h:23-23 ───
[maintainability · medium] The `32768` normal/long threshold is hard-coded here AND independently
hard-coded in `plan.cpp` (`std::min(visible, 32767)` / `std::max(low, 32768)`) with no shared
constant. `live()` selects the target from `visible` alone while `interval_capacity` derives the

used by the kernels and the `capacity` reserved by capture silently diverge, which can under-size

─── src/ops/softmax_attention/dense/causal_cache/bf16/split_policy.h:25-25 ───
[maintainability · medium] `64` is the minimum keys-per-split and is duplicated a few lines below as
`target * 64` (and again in `interval_capacity`'s guard), while `CausalKvPartition` expresses the
same quantity as `1 << key_shift`. Hard-coding this magic number in two coupled places is fragile:
`interval_capacity`'s `boundary >= target * 64` guard is only equivalent to `m*R >= 64`, so if the
minimum keys is ever changed in one place the boundary/plateau guard silently becomes incorrect.
Define a single named constant for the minimum keys-per-split and reuse it.



─── src/ops/softmax_attention/dense/causal_cache/bf16/split_policy.h:35-35 ───
[bug · medium] The fallback derives the target internally from `live(high)`, i.e. from the `high >=
32768` threshold, and completely ignores the `target` argument that was used to compute `boundary`
above. The function is therefore only correct when the caller passes a `target` that matches the
threshold-derived one (the current callers in `plan.cpp` happen to do so via the 32767/32768 split).
Consider resolving the target from `high` for both computations, or documenting/asserting this
precondition so a mismatched caller cannot silently return a wrong capacity.



─── src/ops/softmax_attention/dense/causal_cache/bf16/split_policy.h:26-26 ───
[bug · low] For `visible == 0`, `ceil_div(0, keys)` yields `splits == 0` while `keys_per_split ==

loops `split < active_split_count`), so a zero split count silently skips all work instead of
producing a defined result. The current callers only ever pass `window >= 1`, but the precondition
is not documented or enforced. Either document the `visible >= 1` contract or clamp the result to at
least 1.



─── src/ops/softmax_attention/dense/causal_cache/fp8/instances.h:9-10 ───

─── src/ops/softmax_attention/dense/causal_cache/fp8/instances.h:16-16 ───
[maintainability · low] `DChunk` is selected by a hard-coded head-count comparison and silently
falls back to `64` for any geometry other than `QHeads == 24`. This value is not free: the merge
kernel constrains `DChunk <= 256` and uses it as the per-block d-span (`grid.y = div_up(kHeadDim,
DChunk)` in `causal_merge.cuh`), so an unexpected geometry would get a silently mis-tuned (or, if
the bound changes, invalid) schedule. Consider documenting why 256 is chosen only for 24 heads, or
asserting the supported head counts.



─── src/ops/sparse_moe/small_t/sparse_moe_small_t.h:48-48 ───
[maintainability · medium] The literal `8` here silently encodes `kTopK` (the number of routed
experts), which is defined independently as `kTopK = 8` in `sparse_moe_small_t_kernels.cu` and used
by the S2 kernel to index `token_ids + token * kTopK` / `token_alpha + token * kTopK`. If top-k is
ever changed in the kernel, this allocation will no longer match and the kernels will read/write out
of bounds with no compile-time error. Consider exposing a single shared constant (e.g.
`kSparseMoeSmallTTopK = 8`) in this header and using it here and in the kernels instead of repeating
the literal.



─── src/ops/sparse_moe/small_t/sparse_moe_small_t.h:54-54 ───
[maintainability · low] Two issues with this line: (1) the literal `9` duplicates `kTopK + 1` from
the kernels; (2) the declared shape `{512, 9 * tokens}` contradicts the documented layout directly
above ("each token reuses its [9,512] region"). The kernels actually index per token as `token *
(kTopK + 1) * kIntermediate` (i.e. `[tokens][9][512]`, stride 4608), so the natural declaration is
`{9 * tokens, 512}`. The total element count is identical today, but the mismatched shape invites a
future size/index computation to be wrong. Prefer a named constant and the layout-consistent
ordering.



─── src/ops/sparse_moe/small_t/sparse_moe_small_t.h:46-46 ───
[maintainability · low] Unlike `sparse_moe_uses_small_t` / `sparse_moe_small_t_workspace_bytes`
(which validate `kSparseMoeSmallTMin <= tokens <= kSparseMoeSmallTMax`), this helper performs no
`tokens` validation and is not marked `[[nodiscard]]` even though every other declaration in this
header is. A direct call with a non-positive token count would compute a non-positive
`assignments`/`tokens` shape and produce undefined allocation behaviour. Please document the
precondition (or add a guard) and mark the return `[[nodiscard]]` for consistency with the rest of
the API.



─── src/ops/wrapper/add_bias.cpp:32-32 ───
[maintainability · low] This `checked_numel(bias)` call is dead validation: the shape check
immediately below already requires `bias` to be `[x.ne[0], 1, 1, 1]`, and since `checked_numel(x)`
has already guaranteed `x.ne[0] >= 0`, this call can never throw (`bias.ne[0] < 0` would already
fail the `bias.ne[0] != x.ne[0]` comparison; the product `bias.ne[0] * 1 * 1 * 1` cannot overflow).
Worse, if it ever did throw, it would surface the unlabeled "negative dimension" message instead of
the descriptive shape error. Consider removing it (or moving it after the shape check if an explicit
numel validation is desired).



─── src/ops/wrapper/add_bias.cpp:40-42 ───
[bug · low] The public contract in `include/ninfer/ops/add_bias.h` states "`bias` must not overlap
`x`", but the wrapper never enforces it. Overlapping storage silently produces wrong results because
the kernels read `bias` while writing `x` (e.g. `add_bias(t, t, stream)` on a `[D,1,1,1]` tensor
passes every check here and doubles the values). Since this is the validation boundary, consider
rejecting overlapping buffers (compare the `[data, data+bytes())` ranges of the two BF16 tensors)
rather than relying solely on the documented precondition.



─── src/ops/sparse_moe/small_t/sparse_moe_small_t_plan.cpp:72-72 ───
[bug · medium] The Q5 crossover (`tokens <= 17`) is inconsistent with the mechanism the comment
above describes and with the sibling Q6 crossover (`tokens <= 11`) used in the `else` branch just
below. Rows2's value is covering weight-read latency with extra blocks, and Q5 down weights carry
strictly less traffic than Q6 (5.25 vs 6.25 b/w), so Rows2 should stop winning *earlier* for Q5, not
later. A crossover that is larger than Q6's means Rows2 is selected for T in (11,17] where Rows4 is
the measured-better schedule for the lighter codec. This looks like a stale/typo'd threshold; it
should be below the Q6 value (previously 5).

+                            : tokens <= 5  ? SparseMoeSmallTD4Schedule::Rows2

─── src/ops/sparse_moe/decode/sparse_moe_decode.h:35-36 ───
[maintainability · medium] The workspace geometry is hard-coded here ({8} = kTopK, {1} = one token,

─── src/ops/sparse_moe/decode/sparse_moe_decode.h:37-37 ───

─── src/ops/sparse_moe/decode/sparse_moe_decode.h:15-16 ───
[maintainability · low] The two `SparseMoeSmallTD*Schedule` enums are only forward-declared here,

─── src/ops/softmax_attention/dense/causal_cache/fp8/plan.cpp:34-34 ───

─── src/ops/softmax_attention/dense/causal_cache/k8v4/instances.h:11-14 ───
[maintainability · low] The warp count is encoded as a chain of magic numbers with an unbounded
fallback (`… : 8`). It is only correct because the kernel side enforces `static_assert(Wc > RowTiles

the `<= 64` bound above is ever relaxed, the silent `8` fallback for `kRowTiles >= 5` would break
`Wc % RowTiles == 0` in a confusing way. This is also written differently from the sibling instances
(`int8`/`nvfp4` use `kRowTiles <= 2 ? 8 : 4 * kRowTiles`), which invites copy/paste drift. Suggest
the self-documenting equivalent form and an explicit invariant assert.

-     using Schedule                 = K8V4KvGroupedMmaSchedule<Tokens,
-                                               kRowTiles == 4   ? 16
-                                                               : kRowTiles == 3 ? 12
-                                                                                : 8,
+     // Warps must satisfy `Wc > kRowTiles && Wc % kRowTiles == 0` for the PV partition below.
+     static constexpr int kWarps = kRowTiles <= 2 ? 8 : 4 * kRowTiles;
+     static_assert(kWarps > kRowTiles && kRowTiles > 0 && kWarps % kRowTiles == 0);
+     using Schedule                 = K8V4KvGroupedMmaSchedule<Tokens, kWarps,


─── src/ops/softmax_attention/dense/causal_cache/k8v4/instances.h:15-15 ───
[maintainability · low] KeyTile/MinBlocks are keyed on `Tokens == 1` while the sibling instance
headers for the same kernel shape (`int8`, `nvfp4`) key them on the (GroupSize-independent) row-tile
count, e.g. `kRowTiles <= 2 ? 32 : 64` / `kRowTiles <= 2 ? 2 : 1`. Here `Tokens == 1` implies
`kRowTiles == 1`, but the converse is not true: `Tokens == 2` with the H16Kv2 geometry (GroupSize 2,
`kRowTiles == 1`) selects the 64-key tile and `MinBlocks == 1`, doubling `kArenaBytes` (7*64*256/2 =
57 KiB dynamic) and halving `__launch_bounds__` occupancy for the same single row tile. If that
asymmetry is intentional, a short comment explaining why (e.g. per-token work / split granularity)
would prevent it from looking like copy-paste drift.

─── src/ops/softmax_attention/dense/causal_cache/int8/instances.h:11-11 ───
[maintainability · low] `kWideKeys` keys off `G::GroupSize == 8`, which today is only a proxy for

─── src/ops/softmax_attention/dense/causal_cache/int8/instances.h:13-15 ───
[performance · medium] The non-wide branch is non-monotonic: `kRowTiles <= 2` selects 8 warps, but
`kRowTiles == 3` falls to `2 * kRowTiles == 6`, i.e. fewer warps than the smaller tiles get (and it
only occurs for GroupSize 6, since GroupSize 8 with >=3 row tiles takes the wide path). The sibling
FP8/K8V4 tables keep the warp count monotonic (8 / 12 / 16) and `ConsumerWarpsPerTile = Wc /
RowTiles` here drops to 2, which forces `PVNtPerWarp == 16` and so `acc[16][4]` FP32 accumulators
per consumer warp — a large register-pressure increase that is not present in the other backends.
Please confirm this is deliberate; if not, the wide/narrow formula should probably follow `kRowTiles
== 3 ? 12 : 4 * kRowTiles` style growth, or the magic numbers should at least be documented.

─── src/ops/sparse_moe/prefill/sparse_moe_prefill.h:97-97 ───
[bug · low] `capacity_tokens` is used unchecked. Two boundary problems follow: (1) `capacity_tokens
<= 0` yields `assignments <= 0`, and the initializer-list shapes `{assignments}` / `{256,

growing, which would under-measure the layout and let the public workspace query under-report.
Callers currently clamp to `[min, 4096]`, but the helper itself has no precondition; add an
assert/clamp, or take the clamp as an explicit documented contract.

─── src/ops/softmax_attention/sliding_window/sliding_window_attention.cpp:154-158 ───
[bug · medium] The envelope is only checked for internal consistency (min <= max) and int32
representability; it is never validated against the cyclic cache capacity. The sibling op
`context_softmax_attention` performs the analogous bound check (`envelope.max_context >
logical_capacity` -> throw), and here the equivalent invariant is `envelope.max_context <=
context.capacity` (== window, already enforced to equal `window` above). Without it, a caller can
pass an envelope claiming a context length the ring physically cannot hold; the value then feeds
`sliding_window_attention_resolve_plan`, silently changing the route/split sizing (and thus the
workspace footprint) instead of being rejected. Suggest rejecting it explicitly, e.g. add
`envelope.max_context > context.capacity` to this condition (note `window` is in scope as well).

─── src/ops/wrapper/position.cpp:45-47 ───

─── src/product/logging/pretty_format.cpp:55-55 ───

incrementing the previous one) so the value never reaches the next unit's threshold.
