─── src/ops/wrapper/vision_pos_embed.cpp:17-17 ───
[security · medium] The wrapper validates dtype/shape/contiguity but never validates the *values* of
`indices`, even though the public header documents the precondition "indices ... with values in
[0,R)". This is not a harmless documentation gap: the scalar route (`vision_pos_embed_add_kernel`)
explicitly guards `if (index >= 0 && index < table_rows)`, while the optimized routes selected for
D==1152 (`vision_pos_embed_add_d1152_warp_kernel` / `vision_pos_embed_add_d1152_kernel`) index
`table[corner_indices[corner] * pairs + pair]` with no bounds check at all. So a caller passing an
out-of-range index gets a silently clamped contribution on the scalar path but an out-of-bounds
device read on the D=1152 path — both a memory-safety hazard and nondeterministic behaviour for the
same call. Since this function is the validation layer for the op, either enforce the documented
range here (at minimum for the aligned D==1152 fast path that will be taken) or make the fast
kernels clamp identically to the scalar kernel and state that in the header contract.

overlap check is inconsistent and can silently produce wrong results if a caller aliases the
buffers. Consider adding an overlap assertion before launching.

which is currently pulled in only transitively via `core/tensor.h`. Add the direct include to keep
the translation unit self-contained (IWYU) and avoid breakage if the transitive include chain
changes.

+ #include <cstdint>

─── src/ops/wrapper/scatter.cpp:76-78 ───
[bug · medium] This alias guard only rejects the case where the two base pointers are exactly equal.
`source` and `destination` are required to be contiguous, so a partially overlapping pair (e.g.
`destination` being a sub-slice/view of `source` storage) slips through, and `cudaMemcpy2DAsync`
with overlapping device regions is undefined behavior. Since both tensors are contiguous with known
`numel()`, consider rejecting overlapping byte ranges instead of pointer equality only.



─── src/ops/wrapper/scatter.cpp:27-29 ───

"inputs and destination must not alias", but neither wrapper enforces it, while
`extract_bf16_columns` does. An aliasing call would silently corrupt results. For consistency and
safety, add the same alias guard (or a byte-range overlap check) here.

─── src/ops/wrapper/residual_add_norm.cpp:69-71 ───
[bug · medium] The public contract in include/ninfer/ops/residual_add_norm.h states the three
tensors must be "non-null, contiguous, BF16 pair-aligned, and pairwise non-overlapping". The first
three are validated, but the pairwise non-overlap requirement is never checked (neither here nor in
the launcher). Because `accumulator` is written in place and `x`/`weight` are read during the same
kernel, an aliased `x`/`accumulator`/`weight` region silently produces wrong results. Other wrappers
in this directory (e.g. linear_add.cpp, rmsnorm_pack_tail.cpp) enforce this via a range-specific
`overlaps()` helper; add an equivalent check before dispatch.



─── src/ops/wrapper/residual_add.cpp:16-27 ───
[bug · medium] `numel_allow_zero` returns 0 as soon as it encounters the first zero dimension, so
the nonnegative check is skipped for every dimension after that zero. A shape such as {0, -1, 1, 1}
is therefore silently treated as an empty tensor and the op returns instead of rejecting the invalid
(negative) dimension. The sibling `residual_add_norm.cpp` explicitly fixed this by scanning all four
dims for negativity with a `has_zero` flag before returning zero; align this helper with that
behavior.

-     std::int64_t total = 1;
+     bool has_zero = false;

              throw std::invalid_argument("residual_add: y/x dimensions must be nonnegative");
          }
-         if (t.ne[d] == 0) { return 0; }
+         if (t.ne[d] == 0) { has_zero = true; }
+     }
+     if (has_zero) { return 0; }
+     std::int64_t total = 1;
+     for (int d = 0; d < 4; ++d) {
          if (total > std::numeric_limits<std::int64_t>::max() / t.ne[d]) {
              throw std::overflow_error("residual_add: tensor size overflows int64");
          }
          total *= t.ne[d];
      }
      return total;

─── src/ops/wrapper/sigmoid_mul.cpp:45-47 ───

never validates this alias restriction, so a caller that passes the same buffer as both `gate` and

tensor documented as `gate` (the test asserts "gate unchanged"). Numerous sibling wrappers (e.g.

the sibling wrappers do.

─── src/ops/wrapper/rope.cpp:96-96 ───
[maintainability · low] The `head_dim != kVisionDim` clause can never be true: both public overloads

─── src/ops/wrapper/layer_norm.cpp:42-44 ───

─── src/ops/wrapper/target_logprobs.cpp:72-74 ───

─── src/product/logging/logging.h:41-41 ───
[documentation · low] `parse_log_level` is a public entry point fed directly with user-supplied CLI

yield a false negative (missed overlap) if an address is near the top of the address space; a missed
overlap would let the kernel overwrite an input tensor. The codebase already has an overflow-safe
subtraction-based variant in `src/ops/wrapper/target_logprobs.cpp`. Prefer that form for consistency
and robustness.

  bool overlaps(const Tensor& lhs, const Tensor& rhs) {
      const auto lhs_begin = reinterpret_cast<std::uintptr_t>(lhs.data);
      const auto rhs_begin = reinterpret_cast<std::uintptr_t>(rhs.data);
-     return lhs_begin < rhs_begin + rhs.bytes() && rhs_begin < lhs_begin + lhs.bytes();
+     if (lhs_begin <= rhs_begin) { return rhs_begin - lhs_begin < lhs.bytes(); }
+     return lhs_begin - rhs_begin < rhs.bytes();
  }

─── src/product/logging/logging.cpp:219-219 ───
[bug · medium] `rendered_width_` is the byte count of the last drawn line (`active_line_.size()` in
`draw_unlocked`), but it is used here to emit one blanking space per byte. On-screen terminal
columns differ from bytes whenever the progress line contains multi-byte UTF-8 (e.g. non-ASCII file
paths) or is wider than the terminal (which wraps to a second row). In those cases the erase leaves
stale characters behind and the progress line duplicates. Consider truncating the rendered line to
the terminal width and/or tracking display width instead of byte length.



─── src/product/logging/logging.cpp:210-210 ───
[performance · low] Clearing the line issues one `std::fputc` per column in a tight loop. Progress
updates can be very frequent, so this results in many small stdio calls under the sink mutex. Prefer
a single buffered write, e.g. build a blank run and `std::fwrite` it, or `std::fprintf(stderr,
"\r%*s\r", static_cast<int>(rendered_width_), "")`.



─── src/ops/wrapper/gemma_moe_router.cpp:22-24 ───
[bug · high] `require_matrix` (and `require_vector` below) only compare the extents; they never
check that `data` is bound nor that the tensor is actually dense row-major. Two consequences:
1. A bound-shaped tensor with `data == nullptr` passes validation: `aligned16(nullptr)` returns
true, so the later alignment check does not catch it either, and a null `x` / `expert_ids` /
`expert_weights` reaches the kernels (null dereference). Note the weights path already checks
binding via `require_vector`'s "must be bound", so the activations/outputs are the odd ones out.
2. A strided view with the same extents (e.g. a permuted `[T,H]` tensor, which is `ne = {H,T,1,1}`
with non-dense `nb`) is accepted even though both kernels assume dense row-major layout, giving
out-of-bounds reads. The rmsnorm wrapper guards this with `is_contiguous()`.
Suggest adding the binding/contiguity checks here so every call site is covered.

─── src/quantize/exl3/pipeline.h:48-50 ───
[documentation · medium] The public API's failure contract is undocumented. `quantize_tensor` throws
(`std::invalid_argument` for `k <= 0 || n <= 0 || k % 128 || n % 128`, and `std::runtime_error` when

─── src/runtime/engine/context_cache/context_portfolio_value.h:140-140 ───
[bug · medium] `owner_scratch_` is a mutable member that `fold()` clears and repopulates on every
call, so `fold()` is neither `const` nor reentrant/thread-safe. `ContextPortfolioValue` is stored as
a long-lived member of `MaterializationPlanner` and `SharedCapturePlanner` (portfolio_value_), so if
those planners are ever invoked concurrently on the same instance this vector is raced. Since the
scratch buffer is fully rebuilt each call, its only benefit is capacity reuse across calls. Either
make it a function-local variable (thread-safe by construction) or explicitly document/guard that
instances are not safe for concurrent use.



─── src/runtime/engine/context_cache/context_portfolio_value.h:67-67 ───
[performance · low] Each checkpoint performs a linear `find_if` over `owner_scratch_`, and
owner-policy insertion additionally does a linear duplicate scan, giving O(owners x checkpoints) +
O(owners^2) work. This `fold()` runs inside a hot planning loop (e.g. the 2^N subset enumeration in
`resource_manager`), so with many owners it becomes a measurable cost. Consider indexing owners
(e.g. sorting the scratch by `owner` or a hash map keyed by `PlanningOwnerId`) to make both the
duplicate check and the per-checkpoint lookup O(1)/O(log n).

─── src/product/offload_options.h:24-24 ───
[bug · medium] `*end != '\0'` only guarantees that parsing stopped at the first NUL, not that the

─── src/product/offload_options.h:23-23 ───
[bug · low] `std::strtoull` skips leading whitespace and accepts an optional `'+'` sign, but the
guard only rejects a leading `'-'`. Inputs such as `" 8"` or `"+8"` therefore pass validation even
though the trailing-character checks elsewhere are strict. Either reject any character that is not
an ASCII digit, or trim/validate the prefix explicitly so accepted forms stay canonical.



─── src/runtime/engine/admission_policy.h:32-32 ───
[bug · medium] `donor_count` is a freely mutable public field sitting next to a fixed-capacity
`std::array`. The accessors `protection_has_live_donor` / `persistent_backfill_is_authorized` are
declared `noexcept` and internally materialize `std::span<const std::uint64_t>(donor_ids.data(),
donor_count)` (implementation `is_donor`). Because `AdmissionProtection` is a plain aggregate that
any caller can aggregate-initialize or copy-assign, a `donor_count` larger than
`kMaximumConcurrency` is not impossible, and the current design turns that invariant violation into
a silent out-of-bounds read inside a `noexcept` function (UB) instead of a detectable error.
Consider enforcing the bound when the span is built (e.g. clamp/`assert` against
`donor_ids.size()`), or hiding `donor_ids`/`donor_count` behind an accessor that guarantees the
invariant.

─── src/runtime/engine/context_cache/seal_window_claim.h:23-23 ───
[performance · medium] Backoff loop has several issues:
1. It sleeps on the final failed attempt, so the caller always pays one extra interval beyond the
intended budget.
2. `kBackoff` is a constant 125us with no jitter, so concurrent planners wake and retry in lockstep
(thundering herd) and can keep colliding.
3. 125us is below typical scheduler granularity (e.g. Windows default tick), so the real worst-case
wait can greatly exceed the intended ~4ms (`kAttempts * kBackoff`) budget.
4. When `try_claim_seal_window()` can never succeed (a session with a null impl, or a no-op stub
that permanently returns false), every call still blocks the full budget before the caller falls
back, adding pure latency on every planning pass.

Consider a deadline-based loop with jittered/exponential backoff, and skipping the sleep after the
last attempt.

-             std::this_thread::sleep_for(kBackoff);
+             if (attempt + 1 < kAttempts) { std::this_thread::sleep_for(kBackoff); }


─── src/runtime/engine/context_cache/seal_window_claim.h:17-17 ───
[maintainability · low] The constructor is declared `noexcept` but `Session` is an unconstrained
template parameter. If any `Session::try_claim_seal_window()` (or `release_seal_window()` in the
noexcept `release()`/destructor) ever throws, `std::terminate` is invoked rather than propagating.
All current implementations are `noexcept`, but nothing enforces this contract. Either drop
`noexcept` or add `static_assert`/concept constraints documenting that the claim/release methods
must not throw.



─── src/serve/anthropic_messages.h:81-81 ───

not be reused afterwards.

-     std::string error(const ApiError& api_error) const;
+     // Terminal: drives the stream to finished and must be the last event emitted.

─── src/runtime/engine/generation_budget.h:14-17 ───
[maintainability · medium] `std::abort()` here terminates the entire engine process with no
diagnostic. This constructor runs on the worker at admission time (`engine_core.h` constructs
`GenerationBudget prepared_budget(summary.effective_output_tokens,

leaves `effective_limit_reason == FinishReason::None`) or an internal accounting error aborts every
in-flight request instead of failing that one request. Everywhere else in this engine, invariant
violations are reported as `throw std::logic_error(...)` (and diagnostics are printed to stderr
before hard failures). Since this constructor is not `noexcept`, prefer throwing a
`std::logic_error` (after including `<stdexcept>`) so the failure can be handled/reported rather
than killing the process.



─── src/runtime/engine/generation_budget.h:25-25 ───
[maintainability · low] This defensive check silently aborts the process with no message, which
makes the underlying budget-accounting bug very hard to diagnose. Because the method is `noexcept`,
a throw would call `std::terminate` anyway, but at minimum emit a diagnostic (e.g.
`std::fprintf(stderr, ...)`) before aborting, matching the engine's "fail hard rather than silently
producing garbage" pattern used elsewhere.



─── src/runtime/engine/kv_capacity.cpp:42-44 ───
[maintainability · low] This overflow guard is unreachable with the current types and is effectively

─── src/runtime/contract/sampling.cpp:52-52 ───
[bug · medium] Only exactly `top_k == 0` is normalized to the cap, yet the Op documents that any
`top_k <= 0` ("no explicit limit") selects the full candidate set (include/ninfer/ops/sampling.h:73,
sampling_device.cuh:148-151). A negative override therefore throws `std::invalid_argument` instead
of resolving to the cap, even though the underlying sampler would accept it; the OpenAI-compatible
entry point does not range-check `top_k` (`optional_int(body, "top_k")`), so a client sending `top_k

─── src/runtime/contract/request.h:46-47 ───
[bug · medium] `CancellationFlagView` stores a raw non-owning pointer to a `std::atomic<bool>` whose
lifetime is only guaranteed by a prose comment. Nothing in the type enforces that the flag outlives
the view; if a caller ever stores the view (or the owning record/stack atomic is destroyed first),
`requested()` performs a use-after-free (UB) that the `flag != nullptr` guard does not catch.
Consider making the call-scoped contract explicit in the API (e.g. name/document it as a borrow
valid only for the duration of the transaction call, or take/share ownership via a
`std::shared_ptr<const std::atomic<bool>>`) and/or asserting non-retention at the call sites.

─── src/runtime/engine/causal_score_core.h:75-77 ───
[maintainability · low] This in-flight-job guard is unreachable dead code. `score()` holds
`call_mutex_` for its entire duration, and the worker clears `job_` (`job = std::move(job_)`)
*before* it runs `causal_score` and satisfies the promise, so by the time a second caller acquires
`call_mutex_` the previous `job_` is always null. The `throw std::logic_error` can therefore never
fire, which is misleading: it suggests concurrent calls are rejected, when in fact they are silently
serialized by `call_mutex_` and each will block on `result.get()` for the full duration of the
other. Either drop the redundant check (and document that `call_mutex_` is what enforces the single
in-flight job), or replace it with an `assert`/comment stating the invariant explicitly.



─── src/runtime/engine/causal_score_core.h:101-101 ───

throughput for a scoring engine. Please add a one-line comment stating that the empty snapshot is
deliberate and why, so it is not "fixed" later into fabricated counters.



─── src/runtime/engine/causal_score_core.h:55-56 ───
[bug · low] Shutdown correctness here relies on an implicit ordering: the destructor only publishes
`stopping_` under `queue_mutex_` and joins the worker, while a concurrent `score()` caller may still
be blocked in `result.get()`. Safety depends on the worker always satisfying the in-flight promise
*before* it observes `stopping_` and returns (it does, because the wait predicate admits a pending
`job_` even when `stopping_` is set, and `job_` is cleared before the promise is set). This coupling
is fragile and undocumented — any future change that makes the worker abandon a queued job on
`stopping_` would hang that caller in `result.get()` forever during destruction. Consider adding a
comment documenting the invariant, and note that callers must not invoke `score()` concurrently with
destruction (a late caller will touch the object's mutexes after they have been destroyed).

─── src/runtime/contract/timing.h:72-75 ───
[bug · medium] `end_constraint_wait()` silently assumes a strictly paired `begin_constraint_wait()`
immediately prior. Two problems make this fragile:

1. If `begin_constraint_wait()` was skipped (or `end_constraint_wait()` is called twice),
`constraint_wait_start_` is stale (0 or an earlier baseline), so `device_wait_ns -
constraint_wait_start_` attributes far more (or less) time than the actual constraint wait, breaking
the documented "subset of device_wait_ns" invariant.
2. If `begin_constraint_wait()` is entered while `phase_` is already `Wait`, its internal
`begin_wait()` is a no-op (`transition` early-returns on `next == phase_`), so
`constraint_wait_start_` is captured *before* the in-flight wait segment has been accumulated;
`end_constraint_wait()` then dumps the entire in-flight device wait into `constraint_draft_wait_ns`.

`constraint_wait_start_` is also never reset after use. Consider guarding the pair with a flag
(and/or an assertion) so unpaired/nested calls cannot corrupt the subset accounting.



─── src/runtime/contract/timing.h:115-116 ───
[maintainability · low] The class comment states the recorder "permits explicit Submit -> Wait ->
Post transitions" and can resume Submit, but `transition()` accepts *any* phase change (e.g. Post ->
Wait, Submit -> Post, Post -> Submit without a `pause()`). An out-of-order call therefore silently
mis-attributes elapsed time to the wrong bucket instead of surfacing the mistake, which makes the
recorded breakdown unreliable. Consider enforcing/asserting the allowed transition graph (or at
least documenting the accepted pairs).



─── src/runtime/engine/engine_core.h:1036-1038 ───
[bug · high] The slot is released (`remove_completed_slot`) *before* `complete_success`. If
`complete_success` throws (e.g. the invariant `throw std::logic_error("observed request completed
without stable timing bounds")`, or an allocation failure while building/`take_tool_calls`), the
request is no longer reachable from `slots_`, so the worker-loop recovery sweep (`fail_all_locked` /
`recover_from_oom_locked`) cannot force-complete it. The consumer stays parked in `wait_for_request`
(it polls forever with no deadline) and `outstanding_` capacity leaks. Either complete first and
release the slot afterwards, or guard the completion so a throw still force-completes the request
before propagating.



─── src/runtime/engine/engine_core.h:1067-1069 ───
[bug · high] Same ordering hazard as the terminal-settlement path: the slot is released before
`complete_success(request, FinishReason::Cancelled)`. A throw inside `complete_success` leaves the
request unreachable from `slots_`, so the global failure sweep cannot complete it and the consumer
blocks indefinitely while its reserved capacity is leaked. Prefer completing the request first, or
wrap the completion so the request is force-completed on failure.

─── src/runtime/engine/context_cache/context_cost.cpp:463-464 ───
[bug · low] `std::isalnum`/`std::tolower` are locale-dependent. With a locale other than "C" (e.g. a
UTF-8 locale installed by `setlocale`), bytes >= 0x80 of a device name can be treated as
alphanumeric and folded by `tolower`, so the same GPU yields a different `hardware_class` than a
process running under the C locale. Since this string is a persisted lookup key in preset files,
prefer explicit ASCII classification (`'0'-'9'`, `'A'-'Z'`, `'a'-'z'`) instead of `<cctype>`.



─── src/runtime/engine/context_cache/context_cost.cpp:611-612 ───

sequence (load -> mutate -> write) is not. Two concurrent calibrations (separate processes, or

earlier one's update. If concurrent calibration is possible, guard the whole sequence with an
advisory lock (e.g. `flock` on a lock file) or document/enforce a single-writer contract.

─── src/runtime/engine/context_cache/materialization_budget.h:64-65 ───

─── src/serve/generation_service.cpp:433-434 ───
[bug · medium] `count_prompt_tokens` performs the same expensive work as a generation request —
media acquisition (including remote URL/data-URI fetch, bounded only by `max_request_bytes`) and an
engine-side tokenization/prepare call — but it never calls `acquire_request_lifetime`, so it
bypasses the `max_concurrency + max_pending_requests` guard enforced for generation. Concurrent
count-tokens calls can therefore run unbounded, driving unlimited concurrent media downloads and
engine preparation, and they are also invisible to the overload/queue accounting. Consider acquiring
a lifetime (or an equivalent capacity slot) here as well, so this endpoint participates in the same
admission control.

─── src/runtime/engine/admission_policy.cpp:68-68 ───
[maintainability · low] The freeze path (`make_admission_protection`) treats **every** active
incumbent as a donor, while the rebind path rejects a request that is a current-epoch persistent
borrower (`request.backfill_epoch == protection.epoch_id && request.backfill_class ==
BackfillClass::Persistent`). The documented invariant “current-epoch persistent borrowers are never
promoted to donors” is therefore enforced asymmetrically: here it holds only implicitly, because
`Scheduler::protect_blocked_head` always calls this with a freshly minted `next_protection_epoch_`.
Any other caller (or a future reuse of an epoch) would silently freeze a current-epoch borrower as a
donor and corrupt the donor/borrower accounting used by the backfill proof. Suggest factoring the
identity/duplicate/ownership checks into one shared validator used by both entry points (or at least
asserting the epoch-precondition here) so the two paths cannot drift.

─── src/runtime/engine/context_cache/materialization_planner.h:1140-1141 ───

─── src/serve/request_log.h:75-75 ───
[bug · medium] `enabled()` only reflects the stream's open state and ignores the `failed_` flag that
`append()` sets once a write/flush fails (`append()` then silently drops every subsequent record and
only logs "request log disabled | write failed" once). As a result:

1. Correctness/observability: after a permanent I/O failure `enabled()` keeps returning `true`, so
callers (and the guard at the top of every `write_*` method) believe logging is active even though
records are discarded — contradicting the class's own error message.
2. Performance: because the `write_*` guards still pass, the full JSON payload is formatted on the
request hot path for records that `append()` immediately throws away.
3. Thread safety: this accessor is called from multiple request threads outside `mutex_`, while
another thread may be executing `output_ << record` / `output_.flush()` inside `append()`.
Concurrent access to the same stream object is not synchronized here and is a data race per the
iostream thread-safety rules; a flag read without the lock has the same problem.

Suggest making the failure state an atomic (or reading it under `mutex_`) and including it in
`enabled()`, e.g. `return output_.is_open() && !failed_;` with `std::atomic<bool> failed_{false};`
so the accessor stays lock-free and race-free.

-     [[nodiscard]] bool enabled() const noexcept { return output_.is_open(); }
+     [[nodiscard]] bool enabled() const noexcept { return output_.is_open() && !failed_; }

─── src/text/grammar.h:29-30 ───
[documentation · medium] The public contract of `masks` is not documented here and is non-obvious
for callers:
- It returns a `std::uint32_t` bitmask of lookahead positions that turned out to be "dead" (see
`dead |= 1u << position` in grammar.cpp), not a token mask. The name `masks` is misleading — it
sounds like it returns token bitmasks, which is actually the `words` output buffer (compare
`mask_words()`).
- The input invariant (which is only enforced in the .cpp) is that `drafts.size() < 32` and
`words.size() == (drafts.size() + 1) * mask_words()`; violating it throws `std::logic_error`.

limitation worth stating.

`lookahead`/`dead_positions`).

─── src/text/jinja.h:43-45 ───
[bug · medium] `regions` and `control_variables` are non-owning `std::span`s stored inside
`TemplateRenderOptions`. Because the element types are const, `std::span<const T>` has the
rvalue-range constructor and will happily bind to a temporary container, e.g. `render(ctx,
{.control_variables = std::vector<std::string>{"bos_token"}})` or a temporary
`std::vector<TemplateInputRegion>`. The temporary dies at the end of the full expression while
`render()` still reads the span, producing a dangling reference (UB) with no compiler diagnostic.
All current call sites happen to pass long-lived lvalues, so this is a latent hazard. Either take
the data by value / by `const std::vector<...>&` (which cannot bind to a temporary silently in this
way), or document the lifetime requirement explicitly on these fields.

─── src/serve/generation_token_trace.h:39-39 ───
[bug · medium] `enabled()` only reports whether the stream is open; it ignores `failed_`. Once a
write has failed, the trace is effectively dead yet `enabled()` keeps returning `true`, so callers
such as `HttpServer::record_generation_token_trace` keep calling `write()` and re-acquiring `mutex_`
on every request (and keep collecting `prompt_token_ids`) for the rest of the process lifetime. In
addition, this `const noexcept` accessor reads `output_` without holding `mutex_`, while other
threads may be formatting/flushing the same stream inside `write()`, so the class's thread-safety
contract is only partially enforced. Suggest publishing the failure/disabled state through an atomic
flag and including it here (do not lock `mutex_` inside `enabled()`, since `write()` calls it before
acquiring the lock).

-     [[nodiscard]] bool enabled() const noexcept { return output_.is_open(); }
+     // failed_ should be std::atomic<bool> so this read is race-free.
+     [[nodiscard]] bool enabled() const noexcept {

─── src/serve/request.h:205-205 ───
[bug · medium] `constrains_tools()` claims to report "whether this request needs a tool-call
language rather than free generation", but this early return reports `true` for requests where tools
are declared and tool use is explicitly disabled — i.e. `tool_choice.mode == ToolChoiceMode::None`
(or an explicitly empty `allowed_names` list, where `uses_tools()` is also `false`). Those requests
are not supposed to enter the tool-call language at all.

Concrete consequences downstream:
- `src/serve/request_validation.cpp:199` rejects such a request when it carries custom stop strings
("constrained tools require model EOS and cannot use custom stops"), even though no tool call can be
produced.
- `src/serve/translate.cpp:322` forces `output.preserve_special_tokens = false` for these requests,
even though the tests assert tool declarations (and any tool-call history) remain in the prompt, so
their special markers are no longer preserved.

Either flip the branch to `return false;`, or, if forcing the tool language for
declared-but-disabled tools is truly intended, document that intent explicitly and add a regression
test (no test currently pins `constrains_tools()` for `tool_choice: none`).

-         if (!uses_tools()) { return true; }
+         if (!uses_tools()) { return false; }

─── src/serve/http_transport.h:36-38 ───
[maintainability · low] Mixing the request payload with synchronization flags makes
`HttpGenerationStream` non-copyable/non-movable (because of the `std::atomic` members), which
propagates to derived types, and the defaulted default constructor leaves `prepared` empty. Callers

makes it easy to pass an empty request to `run()`. Consider separating the transport/synchronization

to the namespace/name restoration must be kept in sync in all copies, and a drift between the
streamed Item and the aggregate Item would only surface at runtime. Consider extracting a shared
helper (e.g. `function_call_item(id, call_id, name, arguments, status)`) used by both paths.



─── src/serve/http_transport.cpp:209-212 ───
[bug · medium] `SseStreamEncoder::close()` is documented as an event-producing method that may throw
`ResponseRenderFailure` **or** `ClientDisconnected`, but this handler only catches `std::exception`.
A `ClientDisconnected` thrown here is therefore misclassified as a `ResponseRender` internal 500
(`internal_error`) and a 500 is attempted on an already-dead socket, instead of being reported as a
client disconnect (499) the way every other phase (`open`, generation, `send_error`) does. A
render/render-close failure would additionally be double-reported: first via this
`response_failure(ResponseRender)`, then via `send_error`'s transport path. Handle
`ClientDisconnected` first, mirroring the other phases.

                  try {
                      closing = encoder->close(outcome);
+                 } catch (const ClientDisconnected&) {
+                     lifecycle->response_failure(
+                         make_client_disconnected_failure(RequestFailurePhase::Transport));
+                     return false;
                  } catch (const std::exception& exception) {

[maintainability · low] The exception object is caught by reference and then discarded, so every

[bug · medium] Exception slicing: `error` is declared as `const nlohmann::detail::exception&` (the

handlers no longer match, and behavior differs from a normal `Json::parse`. Making the handler a

─── src/serve/openai_responses_store.cpp:137-140 ───
[maintainability · medium] This branch is effectively dead code, and if it ever did fire it would
corrupt `put`'s failure-atomicity contract. `owned` is always `lru_.begin()` right after the
`push_front`, and because `retain_context_locked` only adds the `owned_bytes` of nodes that are not
already live (`<= response.context->cumulative_bytes`), the pre-check at the top of `put` already
guarantees the just-inserted record alone fits within `max_bytes_`. So the loop condition can never
stay true once only `owned` remains, making both `--victim` and the trailing
`throw_store_capacity()` unreachable. Worse, if it were ever reached, `throw_store_capacity()` would
propagate out of `put` while the newly inserted record is still in `records_`/`lru_` and counted in
`current_bytes_`, leaving the store mutated by a failed insert (contradicting the "failed put leaves
the store unchanged" behaviour the tests rely on). Either drop the branch, or erase `owned` before
throwing.



─── src/serve/openai_responses_store.cpp:242-244 ───
[bug · low] `current_bytes_` is decremented before `release_context_locked`, which can throw
(`std::logic_error`/`std::overflow_error`). If it throws, control leaves `erase_locked` with the
record still present in `records_`/`lru_` but its envelope bytes already subtracted, and with only
part of its context chain released. That silently desynchronises `bytes()` from the real contents
and can make a later `release_context_locked` fail on the same nodes. Release the context first (or
otherwise make this path exception-safe) so the accounting stays consistent when an error is raised.

─── src/serve/openai_common.cpp:184-186 ───

`anthropic_messages_request.cpp:975-977` and `:1009-1012` reject the request with `bad_request(...,
"cache_control", "too_many_cache_breakpoints")`. A client that sends more than the supported number
of markers therefore gets its cache semantics silently altered over OpenAI endpoints but a clean 400
over Anthropic. Either align with the Anthropic validation, or explicitly document/log the drop.

─── src/serve/openai_common.cpp:20-20 ───
[security · low] `std::mt19937_64` is not a cryptographically secure generator, and
`std::random_device` is permitted (and known on some standard-library implementations, e.g. older
MinGW) to be deterministic and identical across processes. The generated identifier is not merely
cosmetic: `new_openai_response_id()` becomes the session key and the `previous_response_id` lookup
key for stored responses, so predictable/duplicated IDs would allow enumerating other clients'
stored conversations. Consider deriving these tokens from a CSPRNG (OS entropy directly, or
`std::random_device` output used as the ID material) rather than seeding a MT19937 state machine.

─── src/text/grammar.cpp:103-106 ───
[bug · medium] `quoted()` emits byte escapes as `\xNN`, but xgrammar parses `\xNN` as a Unicode
**codepoint** (see `ParseNextEscaped` in third_party/xgrammar/cpp/support/encoding.h) and then
re-encodes it to UTF-8 in `EBNFLexer::Impl::ParseStringToken` (grammar_parser.cc:427). For any byte
>= 0x80 the emitted sequence is therefore the UTF-8 encoding of that codepoint (e.g. 0xE2 -> C3 A2)
instead of the raw byte. Because `reasoning_prefix()` iterates over the delimiter's individual
bytes, a delimiter containing non-ASCII UTF-8 (which is valid and already accepted elsewhere)
produces a prefix grammar that can never match the real byte stream. The same escaping is also
reused inside the negated character class, so both the transitions and the fallback class are
affected. Consider building the DFA over decoded Unicode codepoints (e.g. via `ParseNextUTF8`)
rather than raw bytes, or emitting escapes that xgrammar decodes back to the intended bytes.

[maintainability · low] This terminated early-return is unreachable in practice: the only caller
(`GrammarSession::masks`) guards the call with `reachable && !impl_->matcher.IsTerminated()` and
already handles termination by zeroing the mask and setting `mask[0] = 1`. The redundant branch
makes the intended contract ambiguous; either drop it or document that `fill` is expected to be
called only for non-terminated matchers.



─── src/text/grammar.cpp:253-254 ───
[maintainability · low] `constraint_error_kind()` is invoked before the `try` block, so an unknown
`OutputConstraintKind` throws a raw `std::invalid_argument` that bypasses this function's error
translation and escapes with a different exception type than every other failure path (which all
yield `RequestError`). Since the caller relies on `RequestError` for request-level error reporting,
an invalid enum should be surfaced the same way. Note that `error_kind` is needed by the catch
handlers, so it cannot simply be moved inside the try; consider converting the unknown-kind path to
a `RequestError`-compatible failure or handling it explicitly here.

──────── Project Summary ────────

# Repository Review Summary

Scope: 1,286 findings across 565 files. The dominant signal is not a handful of severe bugs but several *repeated* root causes that recur in most modules. The five themes below account for well over half the comments.

---

### Top Issues

**1. Missing operand-overlap / aliasing validation in op wrappers (correctness, high count)**
The public contract headers almost universally state "inputs and outputs must not overlap", but the wrapper layer — the only place that can enforce it — frequently does not check. When it is violated the kernels read and write the same buffers and silently produce wrong results (no crash, no diagnostic).
Representative paths: `src/ops/wrapper/rmsnorm.cpp`, `silu_mul.cpp`, `sigmoid_mul.cpp`, `l2norm.cpp`, `residual_add.cpp`, `residual_add_norm.cpp`, `compact_kv_rows.cpp`, `mtp_pack.cpp` (both entry points), `mtp_round.cpp`, `gdn_gating_proj.cpp`, `attn_input_proj.cpp`, `vision_pool.cpp`, `add_bias.cpp`, `scatter.cpp`, `argmax.cpp`, `sr/ops/hyper_connection/hyper_connection.cpp`. Several wrappers duplicate a half-copy of `overlaps()` (`candidate_selector.cpp`, `linear_topk.cpp`, `position.cpp`, `prepare_masked_block.cpp`) that drops the null/empty short-circuit or the `begin + bytes` overflow guard — so even "checked" ops have gaps. `dense_kv_ring.h` additionally leaves the *distinctness* of `slots` unspecified, which is an in-order data race.

**2. Exception-safety holes around raw device resources (leaks / termination)**
Raw `cudaMalloc`/`cudaEventCreate`/thread construction happens before the validation that can throw, and cleanup lives only on the straight-line path. A mid-construction throw leaks the allocations or (worse) `std::terminate`s the process.
Representative paths: `src/models/gemma4/program.cpp` (raw `void*` members freed manually in the destructor), `src/models/qwen3_5/execution/qwen4_expert_pager.cpp` (pinned buffer + stream + 6 events), `src/models/qwen3_5/execution/qwen4_text.cpp` (five device allocations), `src/models/qwen3_5/frontend/media_cache.cpp` (spawns `HostWorkerPool` threads in the member init list before validating `preprocess_threads`), `src/models/gemma4/cache.cpp` (`std::vector<void*>().swap(owned_)` drops without `cudaFree`), `src/models/qwen3_5/program/graphs.cpp` (events created before the throwing `time_round`). A second class lives in the KV store: `src/models/qwen3_5/program/storage/kv_store.h` fails to roll back source pins / staged transfer destinations / active reservations on the many throwing paths across `prepare_activation`, `commit_activation`, `prepare_prefix_fork`, `prepare_active_snapshot`, `abort_prefix_fork`.

**3. Integer overflow / underflow / divide-by-zero on unvalidated geometry**
A recurring class of silent-miscompilation and UB bugs where derived quantities (`k * k`, `capacity - 1`, `ngram_size - 1`, `merge * merge`, `y * position_side + x`, `patches()` grid products) are computed in fixed-width arithmetic before the invariant is checked, or a divisor is used without a zero guard.
Representative paths: `apps/quantize/hessian_io.cpp` and `parameter_reader.cpp` (unbounded `k * k` / `end * bytes_per_word` in bounds checks), `src/models/qwen3_5/config.h` (five distinct underflows: `ngram_size - 1`, `conv_kernel - 1`, `compress_ratio` and `budget - 1`, sliding-layer count), `src/models/qwen3_5/config.cpp` (`divisor == 0` → SIGFPE, `prime = base - 1`, `divisor * divisor` overflow, `heads()` wrap), `src/models/qwen3_5/program/planning/graph_profiles.cpp` (`capacity - 1`), `src/models/qwen3_5/execution/ple_gather.cpp` (`row_width_` / `head_moduli` division by zero in a member initializer), `src/models/gemma4/vision.h` (`patches()` product), `src/models/qwen3_5/program/vision_control.cpp`, `src/models/gemma4/image_processor.cpp`, `src/models/gemma4/forward.cpp` (`position % ring`), `src/ops/softmax_attention/common/causal_partition.h`, `src/ops/launcher/gemma_flash_attention.h`, `src/core/host_kv_arena.h`. A related pattern: q4/q5/q6/q8 shape selectors only check the *upper* bound and divisibility, so `tokens == 0` and negative multiples of 4 pass validation (e.g. `src/ops/linear/q4/shapes/n3456_k1152.cpp`, `n4304_k1152.cpp`, `src/ops/linear/q5/shapes/n1152_k1152.cpp`, `n1152_k4304.cpp`, `src/ops/linear/q6/shapes/n1152_k1536.cpp`).

**4. Public contract headers omit throw / precondition / ownership behavior**
Sibling headers within the same module document this and the divergent ones do not; consumers reading only the header cannot tell that a function throws, what it accepts, or who owns the storage. This is pervasive across `include/ninfer/ops/` (cast, gelu, rmsnorm_pack_tail, linear_add, linear_swiglu, gemma_moe_router, gemma_moe_experts, sliding_causal_attention, gdn_gating_proj, kv_cache_append, vision_pool, sparse_moe), `src/artifact/` (reader, binder, materializer, exl3_trellis, formats), `src/quantize/exl3/` (hessian, block_ldl, hadamard, linalg, pipeline, trellis_encoder, ldlq), `src/runtime/contract/` (sampling, execution, request), `src/serve/` (openai_chat, http_transport, generation_service), and `include/ninfer/engine.h` (no thread-safety contract at all).

**5. Unchecked `std::optional` / sentinel dereferences (UB / `std::bad_*`)**
Repeated across the qwen3_5 execution and store layers: `src/models/qwen3_5/execution/draft.cpp` (proposal, dflash2), `eagle3.cpp` (`parameters_.draft`), `src/models/qwen3_5/load/dflash2.cpp`, `load/eagle3.cpp`, `src/models/qwen3_5/execution/qwen4_workspace.h`, `src/models/qwen3_5/frontend/output_session.cpp` (`thinking_control_tokens`), `src/models/qwen3_5/program/grammar_masks.cpp` (`grammar_masks_host`). The `Weight::ndim == 0` / `WeightId` sentinel has the same failure shape in `src/core/weight.h`, `src/core/weight_view.h`, `src/models/gemma4/model.h`, `src/models/qwen3_5/model.h`.

**6. Widespread include hygiene (highest count, lowest per-instance severity)**
Hundreds of files rely on transitive includes for symbols used in their own signatures (`<cstdint>`, `<cstddef>`, `<optional>`, `<string>`, `<span>`, `<string_view>`, `<memory>`, `<utility>`, `<functional>`), and an almost equal number carry unused includes that inflate compile coupling. The pattern recurs literally everywhere: `include/ninfer/ops/*.h`, `src/ops/linear/*/…*.h`, `src/ops/softmax_attention/**`, `src/models/qwen3_5/**`, `src/models/gemma4/**`, `src/artifact/*.h`, `src/runtime/contract/*`, `src/serve/*`, `src/product/*`, `src/text/*`. Sibling headers in the *same module* are inconsistent about which follow the convention — that inconsistency is itself the risk.

**7. Magic-number geometry constants duplicated across files**
A few architectural constants are re-declared as bare literals in multiple translation units, so any change silently desyncs one site: `5120` hidden / `14336` / `34816` (e.g. `src/ops/wrapper/rmsnorm_pack_tail.cpp` vs `detail::Nvfp4…`/`Fp8…` constants; `src/ops/dynamic_grouped_conv/q8/q8_dynamic_grouped_conv_add_plan.cpp` vs `kHidden` vs `kRows`), `kTopK = 8` and `kIntermediate` (`src/ops/sparse_moe/small_t/*`, `src/ops/sparse_moe/decode/*`), `32768`/`64` split thresholds (`src/ops/softmax_attention/dense/causal_cache/bf16/split_policy.h` vs `plan.cpp`), `256` causal head dim, `16` candidate cap (`src/runtime/contract/sampling.cpp` vs `kSamplerCandidateCap`), `25`/`88`/`42` route thresholds, `kRowTiles` heuristic duplicated three ways in `nvfp4`/`k8v4`/`int8` instances headers. Related form: error messages hard-code the *value* of a constant rather than stringifying it (`"at least 20"`, `"[4,131072]"`, `"[2,46]"`, `"512"`, `"128"`).

**8. Dead / unreachable / tautological code hides real gaps**
Two shapes recur: (a) a `throw`/`return` that can never fire, because an earlier guard already covered the condition — this means an invalid input (e.g. an out-of-range `LinearPolicy`) silently falls through to a default (`src/ops/attn_input_proj/nvfp4/nvfp4_attn_input_plan.cpp`, `src/ops/linear_add/nvfp4/nvfp4_linear_add_plan.cpp`, `src/ops/gdn_input_proj/nvfp4/nvfp4_gdn_input_plan.cpp`, `src/models/qwen3_5/program/planning/request_plan.cpp`, and the `plan.family == Tiled` branches repeated in fp8/int8/nvfp4/k8v4 `plan.cpp`); (b) tautological validation where a tensor is compared against values just read from itself (`src/ops/wrapper/linear_pair.cpp`, `mtp_pack.cpp`, `src/ops/wrapper/mul_scalar.cpp`'s private `numel` reimplementation, and the many unreachable `numel() == 0` guards caused by `shape_numel()` throwing on `<= 0`). Both patterns look like safety but are not.

**9. Output streams written without error checks**
Multiple CLI/reporting tools report success after write failures. `apps/quantize/main.cpp` (report.json, sidecar, index), `apps/sensitivity/main.cpp`, `src/runtime/engine/context_cache/context_cost.cpp` (checked before flush), `src/serve/generation_token_trace.cpp` (splits record and `'\n'`, and `enabled()` ignores `failed_`), `src/serve/request_log.h` (same `enabled()` gap).

**10. Silent loss of validation in state machines and translators**
`src/serve/openai_responses_request.cpp` threads `breakpoint_count` through but never enforces it; `src/serve/openai_common.cpp` silently drops client explicit cache markers beyond `explicit_write_slots`; `src/models/qwen3_5/frontend/frontend.cpp` (`merge_stop_policy` drops `include_model_defaults`); `src/models/gemma4/frontend.cpp` (exception types normalized inconsistently); `src/models/qwen3_5/frontend/tool_grammar.cpp` (a `body` local shadows the function's `body` parameter). `src/models/qwen3_5/program/planning/startup.cpp` derives the streaming floor from `max_concurrency` while the actual planner uses a different formula.

---

### Module Hotspots

- **`src/models/qwen3_5/program/storage/kv_store.h`** — the single densest file in the review: repeated findings of exception-rollback gaps in `prepare_activation` / `commit_activation` / `prepare_prefix_fork` / `prepare_active_snapshot` / `abort_prefix_fork`, a reservation-accounting mismatch between prepare and commit, `can_retain_reference` vs `retain_reference` disagreeing, and an O(addresses × page_capacity) `rebuild_checkpoint_protection()` on every release. It also has the same `<span>` include gap reported multiple times.

- **`src/models/qwen3_5/config.h` / `config.cpp`** — five distinct underflow/overflow/divide-by-zero findings on the PleConfig / QSA geometry, plus `divisor` and prime-search arithmetic in the parser.

- **`src/ops/linear/**` (esp. `fp8/`, `nvfp4/`, `q4/`, `q5/`, `q6/`, `q8/`)** — include hygiene (many headers), duplicated shape catalogues across alias/enum/if-chain (`fp8_geometry.h`, `nvfp4_geometry.h`), dead `allows_a4` policy branches (`nvfp4_*_plan.cpp`), boundary holes in shape selectors (upper-bound-only checks), duplicated route thresholds, and asymmetric schedule-uses-mma declarations.

- **`src/ops/softmax_attention/dense/causal_cache/**`** — dead `Tiled` branch repeated across `bf16/`, `fp8/`, `int8/`, `k8v4/`, `nvfp4/` `plan.cpp`s; magic warp/row-tile heuristics duplicated three ways; hard-coded `32768`/`64`/`17`/`9`/`DChunk` values; `independent_tiles` divide-by-zero in `common/causal_partition.h`.

- **`include/ninfer/ops/**`** — uniform pattern of contract headers that specify the numerical formula but not the failure/precondition behavior; several have a doc block attached to the wrong declaration (`attn_input_proj.h`, `offload_moe.h`, `gemma_moe_router.h`) or a mis-typed signature (`linear_topk.h`'s `valid_rows`, `sampling.h`'s `purpose`, `softmax_attention.h`'s `min_tokens/max_tokens`).

- **`src/models/qwen3_5/frontend/**`** — duplicate type declarations (`ToolCall` vs `ninfer::ToolCall`, `Modality` vs `PromptModality`, `PreprocessStats` vs `PrepareStats`), heavy include coupling, undocumented processor/output-session state machines, and the `tool_call_parser`/`tool_grammar` issues.

- **`src/serve/**`** — state-machine encoding via scattered booleans (`openai_chat.h`, `http_transport.h`, `openai_responses_response.cpp`), inconsistent exception handling between handlers (`openai_chat_http.cpp`, `openai_responses_http.cpp`), `enabled()`/`failed_` mismatches in the trace and request-log sinks, and a timing side channel in the API-key comparison (`http_server.cpp`).

- **`src/artifact/**` and `src/quantize/exl3/**`** — error contracts undocumented across nearly every header; `Reader::geometry` and `InputFile::read_direct` mutate shared state from `const` methods; `k * k`-style overflow in `hessian_io.cpp`; magic `510` offset and re-validated per-tile work in `exl3_trellis.cpp`.

- **`apps/quantize/` and `apps/perplexity/`** — header/implementation doc drift (`calibration.h`/`skipped_names` always 0), old-format byte-order bugs in safetensors and the perplexity reference writer, signal-handler unsafe `server->stop()` and a dangling `g_logging` in `apps/serve/main.cpp`.

---

### Cross-Cutting Concerns

- **Transitive-include fragility and inconsistent include hygiene** — pervasive; cite `include/ninfer/ops/linear_pair.h`, `candidate_selector.h`, `src/ops/launcher/sampling.h`, `speculative_round.h`, `src/artifact/reader.h`, `materializer.h`, `src/models/qwen3_5/program/context_work.h`, `storage/draft_context.h`, and `src/serve/generation_service.h`. The mirror problem (unused includes) is just as common: `include/ninfer/types.h` (`<memory>`), `src/artifact/schema.h` (`<span>`), `src/serve/serve_options.cpp` (`<nlohmann/json.hpp>`, `<fstream>`), many `src/ops/linear/**` and `src/models/*/*.cpp`.

- **Undocumented throwing / failure behavior on public APIs** — the same finding appears in `include/ninfer/ops/` (cast, gelu, rmsnorm_pack_tail, gemma_moe_router), `src/artifact/` (formats, materializer, binder), `src/quantize/exl3/*`, `src/runtime/contract/*`, `src/serve/*`, and `src/core/dtype.h`. Usually sibling headers in the same directory *do* document it, so this is a consistency drift, not a design choice.

- **Unsynchronized shared mutable state** — `src/artifact/file_io.h` (`mutable direct_fd_` opened from `const` readers), `src/artifact/reader.h` (`geometries` lazily filled from `const` `geometry()`), `src/core/paged_kv_cache.h` (`mutable` scratch in `const` methods), `src/core/startup.h`, `src/core/arena.h`, `src/core/decode_graph.h`, `src/models/qwen3_5/program/planning/materialization_planner.h`, `src/runtime/engine/context_cache/context_portfolio_value.h`, `src/serve/serve_metrics.h` (`attach()` writes without the mutex that `render()` reads under), `src/ops/linear/exl3/exl3_dispatch.h` (mutable static singleton probe).

- **"Validate after allocate"**: the constructor body validates the arguments only after the member initializer list has already allocated the resource (`src/models/qwen3_5/frontend/media_cache.cpp` spawns the worker pool first), or the derived geometry is used before its divisor is checked (`src/models/qwen3_5/execution/ple_gather.cpp`, `src/ops/linear/q4/q4_a8_plan.h`, `nvfp4_w4a4_plan.h`, `fp8_a8_plan.h`). Same shape in `src/models/gemma4/program.cpp` (arena sized from `draft_tokens` before validation).

- **Dead code / unreachable branches** — `src/core/tensor.cpp` (`elements < 0`, `ULLONG_MAX` comparison), `src/core/host_worker_pool.cpp` (impossible `active == 1` branch, `future_error` mishandled), `src/artifact/materializer.cpp` (tautological `size_t` check), `src/ops/candidate_selector/bf16/candidate_selector_path_plan.cpp` (unused `candidate_selector_path_route_name`), `src/ops/linear_topk/linear_topk.cpp` (`valid_rows` tautology), `src/models/qwen3_5/program/decode.cpp` (multiple `pcur > 0` redundancies), `src/models/qwen3_5/program/speculative/mtp_draft_policy.cpp` (empty-ladder bogus rung), plus the many unreachable throws noted in Top Issue #8.

- **Boolean-trap / flag parameters and missing `[[nodiscard]]`** — `apps/perplexity/corpus.h` (`bool quick`), `src/text/grammar.h` (`observe(bool timings, ...)`), `src/product/logging/startup_log.cpp` (`include_bytes` always `true`), `include/ninfer/engine.h` (`generate`, `wait` not `[[nodiscard]]`), `src/media/decode/decode.h`, `src/models/qwen3_5/execution/vision.h`, `src/product/constraint_observation.h`, `src/serve/request_events.h`, `src/serve/http_server.h` (`bind`/`listen`).

- **Duplicated geometry / accounting formulas across files** — `kv_pages_for_tokens` vs `kv_stream_pages` vs `kv_pages_for_frontier` (`src/models/qwen3_5/program/...`), the sparse-MoE profile classification copied in three plan files, the `5120`/`14336`/`kTopK` literals listed above, the two independent `align_up` implementations (`src/core/cyclic_kv_cache.cpp` vs `layout.cpp`) and the local `checked_mul`/`checked_add`/`align_up` reimplementations in `src/ops/linear/fp8/fp8_format.cpp` and `nvfp4_format.cpp`, the shared `require_*` validators duplicated per ops wrapper, and the `linear_attention_state` shape spelled out in three places.

- **Finalizers / write paths that ignore failure** (`src/serve/request_log.h`, `generation_token_trace.h`, `context_cost.cpp`, `apps/quantize/main.cpp`).

---

### Quick Wins

- **IWYU pass**: add the missing `<cstdint>`/`<cstddef>`/`<optional>`/`<string>`/`<span>`/`<string_view>`/`<utility>`/`<memory>` includes across `include/ninfer/ops/**`, `src/ops/**` launcher and plan headers, `src/models/qwen3_5/**`, `src/models/gemma4/**`, `src/artifact/**`, `src/runtime/**`, `src/serve/**`, `src/text/**`. Remove the obvious unused includes (`<vector>` in `apps/quantize/options.cpp`, `<cmath>` in `apps/sensitivity/main.cpp` and `binder.cpp`, `<set>`/`<sstream>` in several loaders, `<algorithm>`/`<limits>` in engine/plan files, `<unordered_set>` in `openai_responses_state.cpp`, `<nlohmann/json.hpp>`/`<fstream>` in `serve_options.cpp`). This is mechanical and removes most of the highest-volume category of comments.

- **Delete the dead / unreachable code called out in the review.** Most are single-line deletions: tautological `checked_numel` calls (`layer_norm.cpp`, `add_bias.cpp`, `target_logprobs.cpp`), the always-true `numel() == 0` guards after `shape_numel()` throws, unused locals (`(void)h;`, `(void)lane;`, `(void)result;`, `(void)ws;` in `q5_linear_add_plan.cpp`), stale forward declarations (`source_writer`/`program_impl`/`request_plan` duplicates), unused helpers (`require` in `trace_reader.cpp`, `q8_linear_swiglu_schedule_uses_mma`, `schedule_rows` in `q8_linear_add_plan.cpp`, `candidate_selector_path_route_name`, `require_unmasked_round`, `kWritable`, `Bf16KvWriteView`, `kMtpDecodeMaximumWidth`, `kMaximumDFlashDraftTokens`, `KvCacheAppend` dead branch, the `plan.family == Tiled` blocks in five `plan.cpp` files), and the duplicated `checked_mul`/`align_up` locals in `fp8_format.cpp`/`nvfp4_format.cpp`.

- **Stringify constants in error messages** instead of hard-coding their values: `"at least 20"` (`sparse_moe_prefill_plan.cpp`), `"[4,131072]"`, `"[2,46]"`, `"[1,8]"`, `"512"` in `causal_compact_attention.cpp`, `"128"` and `"5120"` in `kimi_delta_attention.cpp`, and the op-name literals in `dynamic_grouped_conv.cpp` and `causal_softmax_attention.cpp`. One- or two-line edits that stop the messages from silently lying.

- **Consolidate the `overlaps()` / `require_nonoverlap()` helpers** — `dynamic_grouped_conv.cpp` already has the correct overflow-guarded version; lifting it and calling it from `rmsnorm.cpp`, `silu_mul.cpp`, `sigmoid_mul.cpp`, `l2norm.cpp`, `residual_add.cpp`, `residual_add_norm.cpp`, `compact_kv_rows.cpp`, `mtp_pack.cpp`, `mtp_round.cpp`, `gdn_gating_proj.cpp`, `attn_input_proj.cpp`, `vision_pool.cpp`, `argmax.cpp`, `add_bias.cpp`, `scatter.cpp` and `hyper_connection.cpp` closes the highest-severity *and* highest-count correctness gap in one change.

- **Add plain zero-guards** in front of the known divisors: `head = (ngram_size - 1) * heads_per_ngram` and `head_moduli` in `ple_gather.cpp`/`config.h`/`config.cpp`, `capacity - 1` in `graph_profiles.cpp`, `k * k` in `hessian_io.cpp`/`parameter_reader.cpp`, `position % ring` in `gemma4/forward.cpp`, `independent_tiles`/`multiprocessor_count` in the split-policy headers, and lower-bound checks in the q4/q5/q6/q8 shape selectors. These are all a few lines each and eliminate the silent UB / SIGFPE class.

- **Add `has_value()` guards before the `.value()` / `*opt` / `->` dereferences** enumerated in `draft.cpp`, `eagle3.cpp`, `load/dflash2.cpp`, `load/eagle3.cpp`, `qwen4_workspace.h`, `output_session.cpp`, `grammar_masks.cpp`, and before the default-constructed `Weight`/`WeightId` sentinels in the model/weight layers.

- **Mark `[[nodiscard]]`** where the discard is a bug: `ninfer::Engine::generate`/`wait`, `SseTransport`/`GenerationOutcome`-returning helpers, `constraint_observation_json`, the translation entry points in `serve/translate.h`, `TemplateOutput`-returning `jinja::render`, the media decode queries, and the `kimi_delta_attention`/`sparse_moe_small_t` sizing helpers that are currently the only un-annotated declarations in their headers.

- **Convert magic literals to named constants at the source** and reference them from both the header and the kernel file: `5120`/`14336`/`34816` geometry, `kTopK = 8`/`kIntermediate`, `32768`/`64` split thresholds, `256` causal head dim, `kSamplerCandidateCap`, the sparse-MoE D3/NVFP4 profile table, and the `draft_window`/`kLookupDecodeMaximumWidth` / `kMaximumMtpDraftTokens` bounds that several guards currently use interchangeably.
